# Security remediation ledger

`LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS` below denotes private local verification outputs. These artifacts are not bundled with this repository.

Scope: all 39 recovered issue families from the original internal scan. The source scan failed with partial coverage; its 112 records are evidence to verify, not 112 independently confirmed vulnerabilities.

Each family remains open until all independent subcases are repaired and tested, or a documented current-code review disproves the finding. Preserve research workflows, agent adapters, approvals, target scope, uploads, streaming, and bilingual behavior.

## REC-003: recognized Python stdin programs (partial)

Actual Bash and native PowerShell fixtures reproduce parameter data being parsed as Python source through default stdin, explicit '-' and alias calls. Bash here-strings and explicit -i after a fixed -c program also execute the marker. The initial Windows interactive case hit a UTF-8 preamble syntax error instead of executing its first line, and its data-reader control retained the preamble (template-python-stdin-baseline.log); neither is reported as a successful byte-preservation or execution-boundary check. An attempted fixture-only OutputEncoding override did not remove the preamble. The corrected fixed reader explicitly decodes UTF-8-sig; the interactive fixture's initial comment allows the next supplied statement to be tested independently of the preamble error. No production encoding policy or existing test assertion was weakened.

A shared original-CLI classifier now identifies recognized Python default/'-' source, explicit interactive -i and unknown pre-source modes. Bash reuses ordered stdin/redirection/pipeline binding and existing parameter-flow checks. PowerShell records original consumer argument words, checks the actual captured application's identity after alias resolution, and refuses influenced pipeline data for those program-reading modes before invocation. Fixed Python programs that read stdin as data and managed functions with the same name remain supported. No supplied bytes are parsed as CLI options by the classifier.

Regression evidence: both production fixtures pass two top-level/12 subtests without skips, including eight prohibited variants and four data controls (template-python-stdin-tests.log). A temporary Go overlay disables only shared stdin classification while retaining earlier source/quote/operation protections. All eight prohibited variants execute their additional marker again; all four data controls pass (template-python-stdin-control.log, expected failure). The corrected Windows interactive control emits a preamble syntax error followed by the injected marker, demonstrating why the earlier incidental error was not a sufficient boundary. Bash/Python runs locally on Windows with cached fixtures; native Unix SDK integration is not claimed from those executions.

Compatibility verification with GOTOOLCHAIN=local/GOPROXY=off, cached Python, disposable config and skopos_transport_admission_20261011 selected: go test ./intercept -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s passed 50 top-level/78 subtests, zero failures/skips, 1.464s (template-python-stdin-intercept.log). go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|CustomExecutorsMaskServerCredentials|CustomOperationsReviewResolvedPlansThroughSDK|CustomConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=300s passed 45 top-level/335 subtests, zero failures and two expected native Unix SDK skips on Windows, 230.156s (template-python-stdin-compatibility.log). Edited Go files are formatted; git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check passes. English/Korean guidance is updated. Logs/control overlays are in LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS.

Limits: this classifies original CLI syntax, not environment startup flags (such as PYTHONINSPECT), arbitrary runtime side effects, renamed interpreters or application-defined reparsing. It provides no OS/network/filesystem confinement. REC-003/REC-022/REC-034 remain partial; the checkpoint remains 36 fixed/three partial families. Full application/frontend/database/race and Linux-native SDK compatibility checks were not rerun. No paid provider/scan, dependency download/install, new workspace/service, deployment, commit/push or global host-policy change occurred. The restricted-runtime choice requested in the earlier preflight remains unanswered and is needed before implementing the platform boundary while preserving the required workflows.

## REC-003: preserve fixed native-source quotes alongside parameter data (partial)

The quote compatibility gap recorded below is reproduced by actual custom-tool/SDK execution: a valid fixed Python -c program containing empty/nonempty double-quoted string literals arrives corrupted for direct, alias and dynamic calls (template-fixed-source-baseline.log, three subcase failures). Existing rendering encoded only parameter-influenced argument nodes, leaving fixed source arguments on the SDK's ordinary PowerShell/native serialization path.

For commands containing template-parameter influence, rendering now also encodes original parameter-independent argument expressions against the captured native application. Fixed literals remain readable in reviewed command text; variable/expression evaluation retains caller scope. Managed cmdlets/functions retain their typed values, and fixed CMD/batch arguments keep their previous interpretation. The original-AST annotation is independent of supplied bytes and retains existing refusal of parameter-bearing CMD/IEX/Python source; it is not a general assertion that all runtime expression results are trusted. Conservative assignment-flow limits still apply.

The production regression checks six actual inline/assigned-source executions across direct/alias/dynamic Python calls, asserting JSON output preserves the supplied value, an empty literal, a nonempty fixed literal and placeholder-looking text. They also inspect source visibility in the rendered review text. A temporary Go overlay disables only the new parameter-independent argument annotation: three inline-source failures return, while all seven source-injection refusals and five data/function controls still pass (template-fixed-source-control.log, expected failure). Those failing inline assertions end each group before its assigned-source iteration; the control is not claimed to demonstrate assigned-source failures. Source/argument fixtures and native SDK execution use local cached Python; no provider or network target was invoked.

Verification: with GOTOOLCHAIN=local/GOPROXY=off, cached Python, disposable config and skopos_transport_admission_20261011 selected, go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|CustomExecutorsMaskServerCredentials|CustomOperationsReviewResolvedPlansThroughSDK|CustomConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=300s passed 43 top-level/323 subtests, zero failures and two expected native Unix SDK skips on Windows, 217.338s (template-fixed-source-compatibility.log). The fixed-source test contains three subtest groups with two native executions per group. Existing command/array/alias/batch/quote behavior, custom-operation/rule review, credential masking and interpreter-source guards remain covered. Edited Go files are formatted; git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check passes. Logs/control overlays are in LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS.

The previously observed fixed-source quote case is repaired for parameterized native templates. This does not prove arbitrary expressions, standalone nonparameterized commands, renamed interpreters or Python stdin are safe, or implement an OS sandbox. REC-003/REC-022/REC-034 remain partial; the checkpoint remains 36 fixed/three partial families. Full application/frontend/database/race and Linux-native SDK checks were not rerun. No paid provider/scan, dependency download/install, new workspace/service, deployment, commit/push or global host-policy change occurred. The restricted-runtime preference remains pending.

## REC-003: native PowerShell Python source boundary (partial)

Actual Windows custom-tool/SDK execution reproduced Python source injection through direct, assignment-fed, bundled-option, attached-source, alias and dynamic-command calls. The original-AST parser now classifies source/module/script selectors and preceding options without parsing supplied bytes. A head annotation causes a runtime guard to check the captured ApplicationInfo's Python executable identity after alias resolution and before argument evaluation. Unknown pre-source option expressions fail closed for influenced Python invocations. Managed functions with the same name retain ordinary data behavior.

The baseline log (template-powershell-python-baseline.log) contains six executed-marker failures and four compatibility-fixture failures. Three controls used a fixed source string containing an empty double-quoted argument that the existing SDK/native argument path misencoded; the managed-function control used the wrong named-parameter signature. The corrected controls use an equivalent fixed print program and the intended PowerShell named parameter. This source guard does not itself repair unmarked fixed-source quote encoding; that observed compatibility gap remains under review. No existing project test or acceptance assertion was weakened.

A temporary Go overlay removes only the runtime Python guard, retaining the new original-AST annotations and all earlier defenses. Seven prohibited cases then execute the injected marker again, including a dynamic execution-option case; five data/function controls pass (template-powershell-python-control.log, expected test failure). The production path blocks all seven while preserving all five controls. Actual executors are used; no provider/network target was invoked.

Full compatibility verification with GOTOOLCHAIN=local/GOPROXY=off, explicitly cached Python, disposable config and skopos_transport_admission_20261011 selected: go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|CustomExecutorsMaskServerCredentials|CustomOperationsReviewResolvedPlansThroughSDK|CustomConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=300s passed 42 top-level/320 subtests, zero failures and two expected native Unix SDK skips on Windows, 209.898s (template-powershell-python-compatibility.log). This includes native argument/array/quote handling, dynamic command and alias capture, expression/pipeline guards, actual operation-review/rule enforcement, Bash Python source checks and credential masks. Edited Go files are formatted; git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check passes. English/Korean guidance is updated. Logs/overlay are in LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS.

Limits: Python stdin, renamed interpreter binaries, application-defined reparsing and the fixed-source quote observation are not closed by this change. No OS confinement or general arbitrary-interpreter classification is claimed. REC-003/REC-022/REC-034 remain partial; the checkpoint remains 36 fixed/three partial families. Full application/frontend/database/race and Linux-native SDK compatibility checks were not rerun. No paid provider/scan, dependency download, new workspace/service, deployment, commit/push or global host security-policy change occurred. The restricted-runtime preference remains pending.

## REC-003: Bash templates reject parameter-bearing Python source (partial)

The original source requirements include original-only substitution and rejection of ambiguous code insertion contexts; they do not, by themselves, require an OS sandbox. A new actual Bash/Python fixture demonstrates a remaining interpreter boundary: a parameter inserted inside Python `-c 'print("{value}")'` can execute an additional marker statement even though Bash quoting remains literal. The initial baseline reproduced direct, assigned and bundled-option variants (template-python-baseline.log). Its marker boolean missed Windows CRLF output; the output itself contains the marker. The corrected control below observes it explicitly.

The Bash original-AST classifier now recognizes Python executable names and protects source/module/script selectors plus preceding execution options, including supported wrappers, combined short flags and attached source arguments. Existing parameter-flow checks also reject assignment-fed source. Fixed programs with separate data argv remain available, including quotes, placeholder-looking text, assignment-fed data, wrapper calls and attached -W/-X options. Classification never parses supplied bytes. English/Korean errors and guidance explain the supported data channel.

Regression evidence: a temporary Go overlay removes only the Python classification branch. Five source-bearing cases again execute the additional marker statement; all four ordinary-data cases pass (template-python-control.log, expected test failure). The production branch refuses those five source cases before execution while preserving all four data cases. Compatibility checks with cached tools and GOTOOLCHAIN=local/GOPROXY=off passed: go test ./intercept -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s (48 top-level/78 subtests, two skips, no failures; template-python-intercept.log) and go test ./server -run '^TestCommandTemplate(PythonProgramBoundary|QuoteContextsRoundTrip|RejectsNonDataContexts|RejectsVariableFedShellPrograms|VariableDataWorkflowsRemainLiteral|RejectsParameterFedVariableNames|.*VariableName.*|Shell.*|NestedShell.*)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s (10 top-level/86 subtests, two Windows-incompatible native Unix SDK skips, no failures; template-python-compatibility.log). Actual Bash/Python data execution was performed locally on Windows; it does not attest native Unix SDK integration. Logs and the reversible overlay are under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS.

Limits: native PowerShell Python source, Python stdin, arbitrary executable aliases and program-defined reparsing still require coverage. No native PowerShell/source confinement or general sandbox closure is claimed. REC-003 remains partial; the checkpoint remains 36 fixed/three partial families. Full application/frontend/database/race checks were not rerun; no paid provider/scan, dependency download, new workspace/service, deployment, commit or push was performed.

## Executor environments mask server credentials (shared isolation prerequisite, partial)

The native SDK baseline exposed controlled OPENAI_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, SKOPOS_PG_DSN and SKOPOS_LLM_PROXY fixture values in both foreground and background child processes, including a supplied override for OPENAI_API_KEY (executor-environment-baseline-tests.log, expected exit 1). Its Windows interactive fixture produced no output because norma v0.4.3 explicitly does not support a PTY on Windows; that observation is not reported as a credential leak or an enforcement pass.

All production session constructors already call ConfigureOperationReview; that shared entry point now applies code-owned credential masks before the SDK constructs its tools/context. The finite reserved-key set is OPENAI_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN, SKOPOS_PG_DSN, SKOPOS_LLM_PROXY, PGPASSWORD and POSTGRES_PASSWORD. Supplied overrides cannot restore those names, and the parent's environment is never modified. Native custom commands copy/filter their tool context before template parsing and execution; native Python preparation uses the same filtered environment for execution and the reviewed environment digest. Ordinary task/proxy variables and explicit TOOL_* parameter data remain supported. This is environment hygiene, not a general secret detector or a filesystem/process/network sandbox.

Actual Windows SDK foreground/background and custom Python/command fixtures passed; the unsupported Windows PTY case is explicitly skipped (executor-environment-tests.log). Native Linux SDK foreground/background/interactive fixtures passed one top-level/three subtests with no skips (executor-environment-linux-tests.log), using a rebuilt Linux test binary and a static Go environment-output helper in the existing container. A temporary Go overlay disables only masking: all four Windows executable-path assertions fail again (one unsupported PTY skip), and all three Linux executable-path assertions fail again (executor-environment-control-tests.log and executor-environment-control-linux-tests.log, expected exit 1). The fixtures observe only controlled fake credential values, retain a benign task variable/proxy setting, and verify parent provider variables remain unchanged. No real provider request was made. The first Linux invocation used unquoted dotted test flags and returned usage; the successful rerun quotes them and uses the same completed test fixture, not another container/service.

Compatibility verification: with GOTOOLCHAIN=local/GOPROXY=off, cached Python, disposable config and existing skopos_transport_admission_20261011 database selected, go test ./agent ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s passed 103 top-level/143 subtests, zero failures and one unsupported Windows PTY skip (executor-environment-agent-tests.log; agent 14.296s, locale 0.400s). go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|CustomExecutorsMaskServerCredentials|CustomOperationsReviewResolvedPlansThroughSDK|CustomConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=300s passed 40 top-level/299 subtests, zero failures and two expected Unix-native-executor skips, 195.946s (executor-environment-server-tests.log). This retains native argument/array/alias/quote/template protections and actual custom-operation/final-rule review while applying credential masks. Edited Go files are formatted; git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check passes. English/Korean guidance and execution-isolation requirements are updated. Full application/frontend/database/race checks and Linux custom-Python/server compatibility suites were not rerun for this change.

Limits: other credential names, filesystem/config-file access, already-created MCP/extension processes, already-running sessions and other execution channels remain unconfined. Task credentials using reserved server names must use an explicit task data channel rather than implicit server-environment inheritance. The runtime preference remains pending; the environment change supports either backend without deciding its platform. REC-003/REC-022/REC-034 remain partial: 36 fixed/three partial families. No paid scan/provider, new container/service/workspace, module-cache edit, dependency download/install, deployment, commit/push or host security-policy change occurred. Logs/overlay/helper sources are under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS.

## Shared execution-isolation preflight (REC-003/022/034 remain partial)

The current SDK process entry points and Docker packaging were inspected before selecting a confinement backend. Actual native probes in the existing test container establish Landlock ABI 7 availability, current no_new_privs=0, and denied user/mount/network namespace creation (Operation not permitted, docker exec exit 1). These are capability/current-state observations, not proof of enforced filesystem/network restrictions. SDK BashEnv inherits the server environment and no sandbox callback appears in the inspected session Options; foreground/background/interactive paths launch local processes directly. No module cache or host security policy was changed.

docs/EXECUTION-ISOLATION.md records the authoritative source requirements, concrete executor integration points and cross-platform acceptance checks. Runtime preference was requested because Docker/Linux and native Windows require different confinement backends. No implementation or family closure is claimed from this preflight. Probe source/binary/logs are under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS. No new container/service/workspace, dependency download/install or paid provider/scan was started. Checkpoint remains 36 fixed/three partial.

## REC-022: admission preserves and validates all supplied target anchors (partial)

The original add_intent baseline accepted all 24 prohibited/malformed English/Korean requests, persisted a direction and invoked the task-revival callback; two valid legacy-target requests also lost their anchors during persistence (intent-target-admission-baseline-tests.log, exit 1, 26 leaf failures plus two failed language groups). Invalid/null/zero/negative/fractional IDs were dropped by pidList. Ignored JSON type errors left partially decoded summaries executable. Unknown legacy fields were silently ignored. This erased structured targets before the worker's new validation could inspect them.

Admission now shares the worker's strict positive-ID decoder, preserves supported numeric/string IDs, combines current/legacy target fields, and requires a task identity/asset policy store for anchored directions. Every anchor is checked before node creation. Successful legacy inputs persist as canonical asset_ids. Single-item shape/type failures return a localized error. Batches decode items independently, keeping valid siblings and index-aligned IDs/errors when another item is malformed; invalid entries do not create nodes or independently revive a task. Parent/lineage, priority, valid unanchored reconnaissance and existing operator policy semantics remain unchanged.

The actual PostgreSQL/API checks cover 32 English/Korean admission cases, including current/string/legacy allowed targets, unsupported ID values, wrong field shapes, blocked legacy targets, conflicting fields, mixed valid/invalid IDs, unanchored controls and two partial-batch shapes. They assert frontier changes, revival side effects, item-index preservation and canonical persisted anchors. Combined with the 44 fresh/resumed worker cases, go test ./agent -run '^Test(IntentTargetAdmissionRetainsRequiredAnchors|WorkerPersistedTargetsRecheckedBeforeProvider)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s passed two top-level/78 subtests (including language groups), zero failures/skips, 4.295s (intent-target-admission-tests.log). A temporary Go overlay restores only invalid-ID dropping while retaining JSON validation, legacy-field support and policy checks: all 14 malformed-ID leaf assertions fail again (plus two failed language groups), demonstrating rejected entries once again create intents (intent-target-admission-control-tests.log, expected exit 1). Original artifacts/source were not mutated by the control. Logs/overlay are under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS.

Full verification: the first go test ./agent ./db ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=180s run passed agent/localization but failed four database tests against the reused disposable transport database: duplicate static profile names and an older company matching the same CIDR (intent-target-admission-full-tests.log). These failures were retained. A new disposable database, skopos_transport_admission_20261011, was created inside the existing test container without changing any service or test assertion. With GOTOOLCHAIN=local/GOPROXY=off, the cached Python, disposable config and this clean database selected, the same command passed 287 top-level/256 subtests, zero failures/skips (intent-target-admission-full-clean-tests.log, exit 0). This verifies current admission/worker changes plus existing agent, asset, graph, company, policy, persistence and localization behavior. Edited Go files are formatted; git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check passes. English/Korean guidance is updated. Linux/macOS/frontend/server/full-application/race checks were not rerun for this change.

Limits: summaries with no structured target still retain unanchored behavior. This change does not distinguish concrete destinations encoded only in text, constrain arbitrary programs, or enforce final shell/Python/MCP/extension destinations. REC-022 remains partial alongside REC-003/REC-034: 36 fixed/three partial families. No paid scanner/provider, new scan worker/workspace/service, dependency download/install, deployment, commit/push or global OS configuration change occurred.

## REC-022: persisted worker targets are revalidated before provider dispatch (partial)

The authoritative source records 29/79/104 require concrete target authorization at admission and final execution independently of optional anchors. Records 46/78 also require filesystem/process and network restrictions outside the model. Current norma v0.4.3 launches Bash, interactive shell and background processes directly; existing review hooks do not establish those OS boundaries. No family was closed from this audit.

Workers now strictly decode all supplied current/legacy anchor fields (asset_ids, target_ids, target_id), accept positive numeric/string IDs, combine fields without letting an empty current field hide a legacy target, and reject malformed supplied values. Fresh, retried and human-resumed execution paths recheck these targets against the current task/global asset policy before creating the run directory, publishing target data, modifying coverage scope or invoking the provider/SDK. A missing asset store or task identity cannot authorize anchored work. The same validated IDs supply worker target context. Unanchored workflows retain existing behavior and remain an explicitly unresolved destination-enforcement case.

CheckAssetsIntercept now verifies every supplied asset exists even without restrictive rules. Missing/deleted/invalid positive anchors cannot disappear from authorization merely because GetByIDs returned fewer records. This also strengthens existing add_intent admission for missing anchors; its permissive pidList handling of malformed incoming values remains separate work. The old worker helper that silently discarded malformed payloads is removed. Refusal messages are registered in English/Korean and do not promote summaries or target data to system instructions.

The original worker baseline reached a synthetic provider and host tool probe in 20 prohibited cases (worker-target-policy-baseline-tests.log, exit 1). The first fixed run exposed a fixture mismatch: persisted HTTP service identity removes a bare-root slash. The fixture now grants/blocks that actual stored identity. Expanded current checks cover 44 English/Korean fresh/resumed cases: allowed current/legacy anchors, revoked targets, legacy single/multiple fields, conflicting current/legacy targets, missing/malformed IDs, unavailable stores, closed database pools and retained unanchored workflows. The targeted selector across ./agent ./db ./locale passed 16 top-level/70 subtests, zero failures/skips (worker-target-policy-tests.log). It also covers existing worker system/user context separation, rebuilt history, review context, operator read grants, asset identity and localization. A temporary Go overlay disables only worker validation: all 32 prohibited cases again reach the synthetic provider and execute the probe, while 12 allowed controls pass (worker-target-policy-control-tests.log, expected exit 1). No real LLM provider or network target was invoked by these fixtures. Logs/overlay are under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS.

Full current-source Windows verification: with GOTOOLCHAIN=local/GOPROXY=off, SKOPOS_CONFIG selecting the disposable configuration, SKOPOS_PG_DSN selecting the existing disposable transport database and SKOPOS_TEST_PYTHON selecting the cached Python, go test ./agent ./db ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=180s passed 286 top-level/222 subtests, zero failures/skips (worker-target-policy-full-tests.log, exit 0). Edited Go files are formatted; git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check passes. English/Korean documentation is updated. Linux/macOS/frontend/server/full-application/race checks were not rerun for this change.

Limits: this dispatch check does not constrain an already-running arbitrary program, enforce actual destinations changed after dispatch, revoke active sessions, or distinguish concrete targets embedded only in summary text from target-free reconnaissance. Unanchored/legacy destination binding and shell/Python/MCP/extension network/filesystem confinement still require implementation. REC-022 remains partial alongside REC-003/REC-034: 36 fixed/three partial families. No paid scanner/provider, scan worker/new workspace/service, dependency download/install, deployment, commit/push or global OS configuration change occurred.

## REC-003: PowerShell pipeline expression evaluation refused (partial)

All eight Windows native-executor baseline cases executed the inert Write-Output SW_PIPELINE_OUTSIDE payload: literal input, Write-Output input, assigned/copied variables, an intermediate data command, a new alias, dynamic Get-Command identity and a module-qualified cmdlet (template-powershell-pipeline-baseline-tests.log, exit 1). Pipeline binding can supply Invoke-Expression's source without an explicit argument AST, bypassing the previous argument-only evaluation check.

The inert original-template parser now marks downstream command heads when an earlier pipeline element contains an original parameter marker or assignment-influenced variable. Influence persists through intermediate commands. Runtime resolution captures the selected command once, explicitly resolves aliases, and refuses the actual Microsoft.PowerShell.Commands.InvokeExpressionCommand implementation before invocation. Names alone do not determine refusal: a same-named function that consumes pipeline data remains supported. Parameter bytes are never parsed as code by this analysis. Existing argument encoding and command binding metadata are retained when a head is marked by both paths.

Focused verification: with GOTOOLCHAIN=local/GOPROXY=off, go test ./server -run '^TestCommandTemplatePowerShellPipeline(EvaluationRefused|DataPreserved)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s passed two top-level/14 subtests, zero failures/skips, 10.482s (template-powershell-pipeline-tests.log). Six positive controls cover direct/intermediate/variable/copied pipelines, a data alias, a same-named data function and a fixed expression pipeline followed by literal parameter output. A temporary Go overlay disables only the new head guard while retaining parser metadata and previous protections; all eight unsafe cases again execute the marker and fail assertions (template-powershell-pipeline-control-tests.log, exit 1, eight marker outputs). go test ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=60s passed 11 catalog tests, zero failures/skips, 0.505s (template-powershell-pipeline-locale-tests.log). Edited Go files are formatted; git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check passes. English/Korean guidance is updated. Logs/control overlay are under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS.

Full current-source Windows verification: go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=300s, with SKOPOS_TEST_PYTHON explicitly selecting the existing Python and SKOPOS_CONFIG selecting the disposable configuration, passed 37 top-level/249 subtests, zero failures, two expected Unix-native-executor skips, 195.577s (template-powershell-pipeline-full-windows-tests.log, exit 0). This includes existing native external/nested argv, managed arrays, dynamic executable/alias pinning, PowerShell quote/evaluation/constructor/assignment regressions and Bash template flow/name checks.

Limits: pipeline automatic variables, function-produced parameter data, function-parameter binding, nonassignment writers, dynamic type/method identities, other interpreters and arbitrary runtime execution channels remain unproven. This is original-AST flow plus a runtime cmdlet check, not an OS sandbox. REC-003 remains partial alongside REC-022/REC-034: 36 fixed/three partial families. Linux/macOS/frontend/full-application/race checks were not rerun. No paid scanner/provider, new scan worker/workspace/service, dependency download/install, deployment, commit/push or global OS configuration change occurred.

## REC-022: recorder aliases retain private checked-origin binding (partial)

The HTTP baseline kept the checked numeric origin but lost traffic capture when the native tool selected localhost instead of the recorder's configured 127.0.0.1 address (recording-alias-baseline-tests.log, exit 1). This demonstrates a capture-path mismatch, not an unauthorized origin connection. The new classification resolves same-port recorder aliases once, verifies the candidate against the actual listening socket, and pins the numeric proxy endpoint before dialing. Actual native socket binding checks listener ownership again. Listener identity is published after bind and cleared when serving ends; unstarted/closed listeners, foreign addresses, unspecified candidates and zoned addresses do not establish ownership. External proxies retain their existing path. URL credentials and the caller's proxy URL are preserved.

Actual HTTP and private-CA HTTPS alias requests now preserve one checked origin lookup, logical Host/TLS ServerName and captured logical URL. Focused checks also cover alias resolution failure, foreign/zoned candidates, live listener shutdown, existing direct/proxy routing and native binding revocation. The new test initially compared a pathless URL with HTTP's normalized slash; the fixture now explicitly requests / without relaxing logical capture checks.

Windows verification, with GOTOOLCHAIN=local/GOPROXY=off: go test ./server ./traffic -run '^Test(HTTPPeer.*|NativeRecordedHTTP.*|NativeHTTP.*)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s passed 11 top-level/12 subtests, zero failures, one explicitly gated database fixture skip (recording-alias-focused-tests.log). That fixture was then run with the existing disposable transport database: go test ./server -run '^TestNativeRecordedHTTPUsesTaskAssetIPPolicy$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s passed one top-level/four English/Korean subtests, zero skips/failures (recording-alias-asset-tests.log). go test ./traffic -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=120s passed 113 top-level/60 subtests, zero failures, one existing opt-in skip, 24.520s (recording-alias-traffic-tests.log). In third_party/go-mitmproxy, go test -modfile=LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/checked-recording-upstream.mod ./proxy -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s passed 27 top-level/92 subtests, zero skips/failures, 8.745s (recording-alias-proxy-tests.log). The temporary modfile uses already-cached compatible dependencies; repository module files were not changed. Edited Go files are formatted and git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check passes.

Limits: Linux/macOS, race, frontend and full-application checks were not rerun. Wildcard recorder listeners recognize loopback alias candidates only; actual client admission remains mandatory. Shell/Python/MCP network isolation, arbitrary extensions and legacy execution contexts remain unproven. REC-022 remains partial alongside REC-003/REC-034: 36 fixed/three partial families. No paid scanner/provider, new scan worker/workspace/service, dependency download/install, deployment, commit/push or global OS configuration change occurred.

## REC-003: PowerShell assignment-derived parameter evaluation refused (partial)

The original native-executor baseline executed Write-Output SW_PS_FLOW_OUTSIDE in all eight tested assignment paths: direct/copy/concatenated expression values, script-scoped and environment variables, direct/copied ScriptBlock.Create inputs, and a nontaken branch that appeared to overwrite an influenced value (template-powershell-flow-baseline-tests.log, exit 1). Quoted insertion remained literal during assignment; later evaluation reparsed that stored value as code.

The inert original-template parser now computes a finite conservative fixed point over assignment ASTs, seeded only by original marker positions. Variable identity merges global/script/local/private spellings while preserving drive-qualified environment names. Copies, branches/scopes and array/member assignment references retain influence without treating a later overwrite as proof of removal. Command argument expression collection now includes influenced positional and attached named arguments, rather than only directly marked string nodes. Existing runtime command resolution and evaluation/native guards then inspect the actual selected command object. The script-block source check also follows influenced variable references. Supplied parameter bytes are never parsed as executable code by this analysis.

Argument handling preserves managed values/arrays instead of forcing them into strings. Native values are encoded element by element, maintaining scalar/array argument boundaries, empty values, quotes, line breaks and Unicode. This is required because wrapping an influenced variable expression must not coerce a previously supported data array into one string. Nine native refusal cases cover the eight baseline paths plus attached -Command:$program. Seven managed-data controls cover assignment/copy/script/environment paths, fixed script-block arguments, arrays and attached -InputObject:$data. Eight explicit existing-Python calls validate scalar and array native argv with hostile-looking text, empty values, multiline and Unicode/trailing-backslash input. All are actual local native SDK calls, not simulated executor stubs.

A temporary Go overlay disables only assignment influence propagation while preserving direct expression/constructor protections and the new argument handling. All nine refusal cases then execute SW_PS_FLOW_OUTSIDE and fail their assertions (template-powershell-flow-control-tests.log, exit 1, nine marker outputs). Production source is unchanged by this control, and no destructive payload/external target was used.

## REC-003: literal PowerShell script-block source parameters refused (partial)

The unchanged Windows native-executor baseline confirmed five actual executions of Write-Output SW_SCRIPTBLOCK_OUTSIDE: short/fully qualified ScriptBlock.Create, constructor operands using concatenation or an intervening string cast, and dot-sourced construction. The refusal test explicitly requires both an error result and absence of parameter text, so all eight baseline forms fail its contract, but only five execute the marker. An initially proposed positive fixture used an unsupported string-to-script-block cast; it was corrected to cast an actual literal script-block object, without changing any production assertion or weakening refusal criteria.

Eight refusal cases now stop before native execution or parameter reflection. ## REC-003: parameter-derived Bash variable program inputs refused (partial)

The Bash renderer now follows possible parameter data through parsed assignments, exported declarations, arrays, loop variables and command substitutions before checking recognized eval/-c/stdin program inputs. It computes a conservative fixed point over variable definitions, merging scopes/branches rather than incorrectly treating a later assignment in an unexecuted branch as a guaranteed overwrite. Indirect variable expansion is conservatively influenced when parameter-derived variables exist. Quoted fixed program text can contain a deferred expansion invisible to the outer AST: the existing nonexecuting literal-word decoder exposes that trusted text to a bounded secondary parse (at most 16 levels), checking tainted references without parsing supplied parameter bytes. The traversal retains an earlier tainted reference when later siblings are unrelated. Recognized code/option/stdin sinks now use this variable-flow predicate, while literal insertion continues to parse only original placeholders once.

TestCommandTemplateRejectsVariableFedShellPrograms covers 17 unsafe forms: direct/quoted/escaped eval, exported data inside a fixed nested Bash program, bash/sh -c, assignment chains, command substitutions including an unrelated later sibling, arrays, exported variables, loops, pipeline/stdin/group routing, indirect expansion and a nonexecuted conditional overwrite. Each is refused before execution. The existing Linux native executor fixture now also exercises three variable-fed refusals and an assigned-data positional control. A temporary Go overlay changes only parameterFlow back to the previous direct-placeholder predicate. ## REC-003: declared command parameters enforced at native handler (partial)

The source-requirement audit revisited retained REC-003 records 3, 62, 72 and 107, reading only their summary/rootCause/remediation/remediationTests. Record 3 explicitly requires rejecting undeclared parameters when shell templates remain. buildCustomToolForLanguage previously decoded arbitrary JSON keys and passed them to runCommandTool regardless of the operator's declared schema. ## Proxy lifecycle compatibility verified after REC-022 integration

The previously documented full vendored-proxy failures are now resolved. The HTTP/1 origin-close path used TCP CloseRead on the downstream socket. On Windows an upstream idle timeout could leave that connection reusable, cancel a subsequent request context and return an empty error response; the downstream disconnect callback also failed to arrive. Closing it immediately during an active response would instead truncate response framing. The proxy now tracks owned HTTP/1 connection state: an origin closure fully closes an idle downstream connection, while an active response finishes and the HTTP server's StateIdle callback closes it only after flushing the response and final chunk. Entry and intercepted-TLS HTTP servers share that state hook. HTTP/2 retains its existing separate connection-close behavior; an externally supplied HTTP/2 ServeConn connection is not cast to an owned attacker connection. No keepalive feature was disabled and no failed response expectation was relaxed.

The old untrusted-certificate test required the literal phrase certificate is not trusted, although the observed error was x509: certificate signed by unknown authority. It now requires errors.As(..., x509.UnknownAuthorityError), preserving the trust-failure requirement across platform/runtime wording. Its existing positive TLS control remains unchanged. TestOriginClosureFinishesChunkedHTTPResponse deliberately closes the actual origin socket at response-body EOF, forces downstream chunked framing and makes two HTTP or HTTPS calls. Both complete with status 200, intact body and valid final chunk; both origin closures are exercised, and the second call reconnects safely. A temporary source overlay changes only the defer-while-active decision into immediate closure: both protocol cases fail with EOF (proxy-lifecycle-premature-close-tests.log, expected exit 1). Production source is not altered by the overlay.

## REC-022: native recording proxy preserves checked destination and capture (partial)

The earlier dial-primitive checkpoint below is now integrated into native custom HTTP execution. When an enabled IP/CIDR policy requires a checked peer and the selected proxy is the configured recorder, httpPeerTransport resolves the origin once, chooses the first approved numeric address and privately binds its actual TCP connection before writing HTTP bytes. BindNativeHTTPConnection verifies the recorder endpoint and native client admission, rejects duplicate socket ownership, and bounds its registry to 4096 entries. The proxy keeps logical Host/SNI/capture URLs, rechecks the original request/task context before dispatch and retains revoked bindings until client disconnection; close/cancellation cannot silently turn buffered requests into ordinary DNS dialing. Distinct typed connection/request context keys replace potentially aliased pointers to zero-sized structs, preserving the binding when a forwarded request adds metadata. Bound native requests require interception even for a host previously marked for transparent passthrough. Changed Host, alternate clients, websocket dialers and a lost binding fail closed. Unbound clients retain existing routing and capture behavior.

TestNativeHTTPBindingPreservesCaptureAndAuthority uses real loopback HTTP/TLS origins and the actual recorder: its six cases prove response/header/capture-body/capture-URL preservation, original TLS SNI, interception despite a prior passthrough mark, and zero target HTTP requests for alternate transport, changed Host or policy revoked by an addon just before dispatch. TestNativeHTTPBindingRejectsInvalidOwnershipAndCancellation rejects a foreign socket and duplicate registration, then deliberately writes an HTTP request over an already-connected socket after its bound context is cancelled; the proxy returns 502 and the target receives no request. Successful exchanges also verify registry cleanup after disconnection. TestRecordedHTTPPeerTransportUsesCheckedNumericOrigin checks the actual server transport's HTTP/TLS route, one origin lookup per request/redirect, blocked IP, pre-bind revocation and DNS failure. TestNativeRecordedHTTPUsesTaskAssetIPPolicy exercises runHTTPTool with real disposable PostgreSQL asset rules in both languages: automatic HTTPS recording and explicitly selected HTTP recording preserve the allowed response/evidence, while a subsequently blocked IPv4 peer reaches neither the target nor capture. The IPv6 localhost peer is independently blocked so it cannot evade that control.

Verification, with GOTOOLCHAIN=local and GOPROXY=off: go test ./traffic ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s passed 123 Windows top-level tests (112 traffic, 11 locale), zero failures and one existing opt-in proxy-admission skip (native-recording-traffic-windows-tests.log). The rebuilt Linux/amd64/CGO_ENABLED=0 traffic binary passed the full Linux traffic suite with that same opt-in skip (native-recording-traffic-linux-tests.log). go test ./server -run '^Test(NativeRecordedHTTPUsesTaskAssetIPPolicy|RecordedHTTPPeerTransportUsesCheckedNumericOrigin|HTTPPeer.*|PinnedHTTPProxy.*|RecordingHTTPProxyURL.*|HTTPProxyDestinationIPRestrictions|DefaultHTTPDeleteRulesBlockSDKRequestsBeforeTransport)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s passed 15 top-level/37 subtests on Windows and the rebuilt Linux binary, zero failures/skips (native-recording-server-windows-tests.log, 5.035s; native-recording-server-linux-tests.log). This includes actual seeded DELETE-policy SDK/transport guards. Six checked-upstream proxy tests pass using the temporary cached application-version module file (native-recording-proxy-focused-tests.log, 1.256s), including the forwarded-context regression. A source overlay removes only binding.check at proxy dispatch: the revocation control fails as expected with status 200 and one real inert target request (native-recording-no-dispatch-policy-tests.log, exit 1). Production source is unchanged by the overlay. English/Korean behavior documentation, localized binding errors and the vendored upgrade notes are updated; gofmt and git diff --check pass.

The current full vendored proxy suite still fails in the same legacy certificate-error-text and immediate keepalive-close/disconnect cases (native-recording-proxy-full-tests.log, 8.465s); the preceding binding-disabled control reproduced these failure locations/messages. The full vendored suite is therefore not reported green, and these independent failures remain unresolved. Full application/frontend, macOS, concurrent OS-level socket reuse, private hostname recorder endpoints beyond localhost, and attestation of an external egress proxy's ultimate peer were not verified. REC-022 remains partial for proxy-only DNS, aliases of the recorder endpoint, arbitrary shell/Python/MCP/extensions, legacy intent/runtime boundaries and general network isolation. REC-003 and REC-034 remain partial for their broader interpreter/runtime requirements. ## REC-004: source requirements and native request boundary verified (fixed)

REC-004 contains only retained source record 4. Its root cause is automatic allowance of shell-equivalent curl DELETE spellings while the judge is disabled. Its required checks are (1) nonexecuting decisions deny quoted DELETE and --request=DELETE along with unquoted forms, and (2) blocked operations never send target requests. The existing decoded-argv/method projection covers those equivalent forms and the shared guard checks retain explicit operator allow priority. The new SDK/transport regression directly proves the second requirement using the actual seeded rules, resolved custom-tool binding, production agent hooks and native HTTP execution path. The default disabled judge does not permit a lexical miss: recognized equivalents deny, and unmatched general shell/script capability requires independent human authorization while restrictive input policy is enabled.

TestDefaultHTTPDeleteRulesBlockSDKRequestsBeforeTransport temporarily enables only the two actual seeded HTTP DELETE policies in the existing disposable database, saving and restoring every prior enabled state. Its 24 cases cover both languages and model judge on/off: three curl spellings (quoted method, --request=DELETE, quoted executable/option/method fragments), native DELETE, whitespace/case-normalized native DELETE and native GET. All 20 blocked cases match a real seeded deny rule, never invoke the synthetic always-allow judge, produce one not_executed denial audit and send zero requests to the local HTTP listener. All four GET controls send exactly one GET; the enabled synthetic judge is invoked only for those permitted native HTTP calls. This positive control proves the listener/executor path works and avoids passing merely because execution is broken. No curl process runs for denied command cases.

An isolated Go overlay removes only native HTTP's agent.ReviewOperation call and runs the native-delete/judge=false/en case. It fails as expected after a real DELETE reaches the loopback listener (delete-boundary-no-review-control-tests.log: requests=1, want=0; exit 1). This is a live inert local request, not a destructive target interaction or a reproduction against a remote service. Production source was not changed by the overlay. The current runHTTPTool normalizes the actual method, reviews the prepared request before client.Do, and reviews each redirected request's effective method/body/destination through CheckRedirect. Its request-boundary enforcement is therefore based on actual native request state rather than a shell spelling. The existing redirect SDK regression remains green.

Verification: GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^TestDefaultHTTPDeleteRulesBlockSDKRequestsBeforeTransport$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=60s with the existing disposable transport PostgreSQL/config passed one top-level/24 subtests on Windows, zero failures/skips (delete-boundary-sdk-windows-tests.log, 2.341s). The rebuilt Linux/amd64/CGO_ENABLED=0 server binary passed the same 24 cases, zero failures/skips (delete-boundary-sdk-linux-tests.log). GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^Test(DefaultHTTPDeleteRulesBlockSDKRequestsBeforeTransport|CustomHTTPRedirectsReviewEffectiveMethodBodyAndDestination|CustomOperationsReviewResolvedPlansThroughSDK|CustomConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule|UnconfinedCustomCommandRequiresHumanBeforeNativeDispatch)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s with the existing explicit Python interpreter passed five top-level/86 subtests on Windows, zero failures/skips (delete-boundary-sdk-integration-tests.log, 9.179s). This retains the 48-case optional-judge/resolved-plan matrix, actual-native human-authority cases and redirect semantics. Logs/binary/overlay are under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS. Existing 64-case policy suites on both platforms and decoded-argv guard tests were unchanged by this test-only audit. Full server/frontend suites were not rerun; gofmt and git diff --check passed.

Closure scope: enabled selected-tool interception and its configured priority/authorization semantics. A matching higher-priority operator allow, a person's grant of general runtime authority, or explicit disabling of interception remains an intentional authorization/configuration choice, not a guarantee that general shell clients can never issue DELETE. This closure does not advertise a universal HTTP-method firewall or OS/network confinement for arbitrary approved programs. Those broader confinement requirements remain in REC-022/REC-034. Both explicit source-record checks for REC-004 are now satisfied without removing supported research tools, native GET behavior, approval controls or redirect handling.

Current checkpoint: 36 fixed/three partial families (REC-003, REC-022, REC-034). No paid scanner/provider, dependency install/download, deployment, commit/push or global OS change occurred.

## REC-022: checked recording-proxy dial primitive (partial, integration pending)

The vendored proxy now offers an optional connection-bound numeric upstream selector. It preserves the logical HTTP Host and origin TLS ServerName, freezes the checked address/authority/scheme, rechecks the selector before dispatch, and refuses lost bindings, changed Host identity, alternate clients and websocket dialers for bound requests. Direct dialing and upstream HTTP CONNECT use the checked numeric destination. Ordinary connections without a selector retain existing behavior. The application has not registered private native-request bindings: its existing recording-proxy refusal under IP restrictions remains in place. This primitive alone does not close REC-022 or attest an external proxy's final peer.

The temporary module file selects the application's already-cached dependency versions; repository dependency files were not changed. The application traffic suite passed (go test ./traffic -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s; checked-recording-upstream-traffic-tests.log, 23.324s).

## REC-004 / REC-034: unmatched unconfined shell execution requires a person (partial follow-up)

Static command inspection previously permitted literal python/node programs, renamed clients, build tools, functions and ordinary native commands whenever no lexical/projected restriction matched. Nine cases across Bash/shell_open/shell_send/resolved custom controls reproduced that permissive result with a deliberately unmatched restrictive input rule (unconfined-before-tests.log, expected exit 1). A native SDK before-overlay disables only the new fallback condition and verifies that an always-allow synthetic model judge executes echo into a real disposable marker without a human decision (unconfined-native-before-tests.log, expected exit 1). This is an actual local file effect through the production custom-tool/SDK path, not a dispatch-counter substitute. No destructive or remote-target action was used.

MatchNames now requires human approval for every unmatched shell executor when any enabled deny/ask tool-input rule is active. Existing matched decisions retain global configured priority, including explicit operator allow overrides. Known lexical/canonical forbidden operations still deny before this gate. Specific unknown-executable/method/program reasons remain when applicable; otherwise the bilingual reason explains that input rules cannot confine shell execution. The gate does not call the optional model reviewer, and always uses a five-minute deny timeout regardless of an allow-on-timeout model setting. Empty interactive input also requires review, because existing session state cannot be attested from the new input alone. Tool interception explicitly disabled by the operator and policies without restrictive input rules retain existing behavior. Native WebFetch and non-shell tools do not acquire this shell fallback. This changes previously automatic research commands into approval requests; narrowly scoped matching operator allow rules preserve deliberately authorized repeated workflows. It is human authorization rather than OS/network confinement.

Nine compatibility tests previously equated absence of projected forbidden code with automatic execution permission. They now retain their data-role/uncertainty assertions and require the new unconfined human decision, while existing deny, allow priority and inactive/non-shell policy assertions remain. The 48-case optional-model-judge SDK fixture now temporarily disables/restores restrictive rules in its disposable database: mandatory human authorization is verified by a separate real-native fixture, and an optional judge cannot override that policy. Its per-run task IDs are unique and each case has a bounded context, avoiding stale audits from an interrupted run without relaxing the single-audit assertion. Initial native assertions incorrectly looked for synthetic rule names in the joined rule-row name rather than immutable audit provenance, and used completed instead of the existing succeeded execution status; these were corrected to the actual audit contract. Cancellation can return from the SDK before the hook finishes updating its exact pending row; the fixture waits at most one second for terminal denial, retains the terminal audit assertion and rechecks absence of the marker after denial.

Verification: GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./intercept ./guard ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=75s with explicit existing disposable semantic PostgreSQL/config passed 64 top-level Windows tests (50 intercept, three guard, 11 locale), zero failures/skips (unconfined-policy-windows-tests.log). Linux/amd64/CGO_ENABLED=0 binaries in the existing disposable container passed the same 50 intercept, three guard and 11 locale tests, zero failures/skips (unconfined-{intercept,guard,locale}-linux-tests.log). The shared guard fixture now covers 468 bilingual cases across three shell controls, human allow/deny/context expiry, with a nonexecuting dispatcher stub. Matching operator controls and enabled-tool settings are restored.

GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^Test(UnconfinedCustomCommandRequiresHumanBeforeNativeDispatch|CustomOperationsReviewResolvedPlansThroughSDK|CustomConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule)$' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s with the existing explicit Python interpreter and disposable transport PostgreSQL/config passed three top-level Windows tests, zero failures/skips (unconfined-native-sdk-tests.log, 8.293s). The Linux native fixture also passed its 12 cases, zero failures/skips (unconfined-native-sdk-linux-tests.log); the Python-dependent optional-judge fixture was not selected in that container. The real five-minute timeout was not waited out: the tests check denial timeout fields and exercise context cancellation. All logs/binaries/overlay are under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS. Source audit: REC-034 records 46 and 78 share the raw-spelling/no-match-allow root cause. Record 78 explicitly requires catch-all worker/chat approval and no unapproved executor dispatch; this gate addresses that authorization gap, with actual ChatAgent SDK effects and common guard-hook coverage. Record 46 also explicitly asks for OS restrictions preventing filesystem/network effects when detection misses; those controls are not present or proved, so the family remains partial. REC-004 record 4 requires normalized equivalent curl denials and a blocked-request transport check, and recommends method enforcement at a network boundary. Existing normalized decisions plus this gate close automatic arbitrary-shell fallback, but do not claim HTTP method confinement after a human or matching operator allow grants general execution. ## REC-003: parameterized shell stdin programs and descriptor bindings (partial follow-up)

Twelve original regression cases were accepted by the renderer despite placing parameter text in stdin programs: direct/forwarded pipelines, single/multiple-command groups, subshells, here-strings, input-file selection, unsupported shell/wrapper options and last-input redirection changes (shell-template-stdin-before-tests.log, expected go test exit 1). These before tests inspect source acceptance, not executed injection. Four additional cases exposed descriptor-copy gaps after the initial implementation: 0<&0, 0<&3, 0>&3 and group-inherited FD 3 copied through cat (shell-template-stdin-fd-before-tests.log, expected go test exit 1). All 16 now fail rendering before the SDK can dispatch an interpolated code stream.

The new static stdin-source helper tracks inherited descriptor bindings, ordered redirections, descriptor copies/moves and pipeline/group input. Recognized stdin shells and opaque recognized wrapper/shell option forms protect those code-source nodes. A generated descriptor selector conservatively includes possible inherited bindings. Consumer input overrides replace pipeline input; producer bindings are evaluated after its own redirects, so unused inherited input is not mistaken for supplied code. Literal printf/echo supply their call's data; plain cat supplies its effective stdin; opaque producers conservatively include their readable inherited descriptors. Output-only file descriptors do not supply code. The helper does not execute producers, read input files, resolve environment variables or attest runtime command identities.

Twelve real native Bash data cases preserve ordinary stdin, fixed bash -c 'cat', syntax-only bash -n, fixed stdin overriding a parameterized pipe, unused inherited input, FD 3 data, descriptor copies overwritten by later fixed input, and fixed producer input overriding a group's parameterized input. Four further rendering checks preserve output file parameters, overridden input files, command identity queries and data-only stdin duplication. The Linux actual-SDK fixture now also confirms pipeline/here-string code is refused and fixed program stdin data executes literally. This intentionally disallows parameterized stdin code and input-file selectors which choose shell programs; use a fixed program and positional/stdin data that the program does not reevaluate. Opaque producer dependencies are conservative, so some complex parameterized program-producing pipelines may require restructuring. Variable/assignment flow across statements, runtime-defined or substituted command identities, aliases/functions, sourced state, arbitrary other interpreters and later re-evaluation of positional data remain unproved. REC-003 stays partial; this does not establish runtime confinement.

Verification: GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^TestCommandTemplate(RejectsShellStdinProgramInterpolation|ShellStdinDataRemainsLiteral|ShellStdinRedirectionBindings|RejectsShellProgramInterpolation|NestedShellDataArgumentsRemainLiteral|QuoteContextsRoundTrip|RejectsNonDataContexts)$' -count=1 -v -timeout=45s passed seven top-level Windows tests, zero failures/skips (shell-template-stdin-focused-tests.log, 4.311s). A rebuilt Linux/amd64/CGO_ENABLED=0 server binary in the existing disposable container passed twelve top-level tests, zero failures/skips, using '^Test(CommandTemplate(RejectsShellStdinProgramInterpolation|ShellStdinDataRemainsLiteral|ShellStdinRedirectionBindings|RejectsShellProgramInterpolation|NestedShellDataArgumentsRemainLiteral|NestedShellSDKBoundary|QuoteContextsRoundTrip|UsesActualSDKExecutor|RejectsNonDataContexts)|RenderTemplate)' (shell-template-stdin-linux-tests.log). GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./intercept ./guard ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=60s with explicit existing disposable semantic PostgreSQL/config passed 62 top-level Windows tests, zero failures/skips (shell-template-stdin-policy-tests.log). Logs/binaries are under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS. Full server/frontend and the unchanged native PowerShell matrix were not rerun. Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check passed.

## REC-003: nested Bash program and option interpolation (partial follow-up)

The before-change renderer accepted all eight original regression cases: bash/sh -c, recognized env/command/timeout wrappers, eval/builtin eval, and Bash -o pipefail. It inserted FIXED_ECHO; printf SW_OUTSIDE directly into the nested program despite quoting the outer argument (shell-template-program-before-tests.log, expected go test exit 1). That test inspected rendered source, not executed injection. The renderer now reuses intercept's static word decoder, wrapper grammar and shell execution-option grammar to distinguish recognized code/control words from positional data. It refuses mapped parameters in nested program strings, eval arguments, execution options, wrapper control prefixes and wrapped command identities. Unsupported recognized invocations with parameters fail before dispatch. A presumed script filename before -- is also protected because a substituted value could become an execution option. Static nested programs with separately quoted positional data remain available; use bash -c 'printf "%s" "$1"' fixture '{value}'. Error text is registered for English/Korean. The helper does no execution, environment or filesystem lookup.

The native data fixture initially failed on Windows because env -i removed Bash's executable-search PATH. Giving that isolated invocation an explicit /usr/bin:/bin PATH repaired the fixture without changing its exact-byte output assertion. Four native nested data cases now preserve quotes, command substitutions, semicolons and placeholder-looking text literally. The Linux actual-SDK fixture verifies code interpolation is refused and the safe positional-data form executes unchanged. This intentionally restricts unsafe code/control interpolation; fixed script programs and positional-data workflows remain. The same policy applies even inside command substitutions because calls are visited throughout the parsed outer AST. Pipeline/stdin programs, runtime-defined or substituted command identities, other interpreters, sourced program state, renamed applications and later evaluation of positional data are not proved confined. REC-003 remains partial; this is not an OS execution sandbox.

## REC-003: CMD program interpolation rejected at runtime target binding (partial follow-up)

An actual Windows reproduction of & cmd.exe /d /s /c 'echo {value}' with value FIXED_ECHO & echo SW_OUTSIDE printed two separate lines. The existing native argv encoder protected the outer PowerShell argument but CMD parsed its contents again as a program, exceeding the fixed echo template. This is a measured inert stdout-only command injection, not a speculative parser discrepancy (cmd-program-before-tests.log, expected go test exit 1). No files, remote targets or OS settings were changed by the reproduction.

The completed native-argument encoder now refuses parameter interpolation when the pinned runtime CommandInfo resolves to cmd.exe. It checks the actual ApplicationInfo path after alias resolution, before the outer command is invoked; static paths, ComSpec expressions, resolved command objects and aliases receive the same refusal. All parameter arguments to CMD are refused because /c can concatenate trailing arguments into its program too. Fixed CMD programs without placeholders remain available, including when managed data is rendered elsewhere in the same template. Existing batch argument encoding and ordinary native/managed data paths remain unchanged. The refusal message is generated in the request's English/Korean locale. This deliberately restricts an unsafe code-interpolation form; use direct literal native arguments, managed PowerShell argument data, or the Python script tool's JSON/environment input instead. Arbitrary nonstandard parsers, secondary interpreters/code evaluation, renamed interpreter applications and ambient batch modes are not proved confined by this check. REC-003 remains partial rather than advertising a universal argv/code-safety guarantee.

The rebuilt Linux/amd64/CGO_ENABLED=0 server binary passed six Bash/rendering regressions with the actual SDK executor (cmd-program-linux-tests.log); no Windows CMD behavior is claimed on Linux. ## REC-024: bounded retained exchange count and source-finding closure

New capture now has a store-wide retained-row ceiling, default 100000, configured with positive SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_MAX_EXCHANGES before startup (also passed through Compose). checkCaptureAdmission counts persisted exchanges under the recorder write lock before body spills or index transactions; host changes, concurrent calls and restarts cannot reset it. Reaching capacity skips recording and reports the existing dropped-capture counter while forwarding the target response. Existing evidence is retained rather than automatically evicted; delete/purge releases capacity. Opening a store with a lowered limit keeps older evidence and blocks new capture until capacity is available. Explicit authenticated archive restoration remains an evidence-preservation operation under its existing storage/import budgets and can restore more rows than this capture ceiling; restored rows count against all later capture admission. This is bounded retention by count, not automatic expiration by age.

Closure audit against recovered record 32 (REC-024): its source trace is sink.Response -> Traffic.record -> spill -> writeBlob/SQLite, and its demonstrated threat is hostile target responses accumulating persistent capture data during normal capture. The present recorder has physical blob/index/WAL ceilings, bounded native journal/shared scratch allowances, per-exchange/per-host byte accounting, this retained-row ceiling, native free-space admission/growth checks, atomic rollback/new-blob cleanup and capture-off persistence enforcement. No per-exchange filesystem trees are created; response bodies spill only through the budgeted blob writer. MITM leaf certificates are generated/cached in memory, not persisted per target (third_party/go-mitmproxy/cert/self_sign_ca.go GetCert/DummyCert); fixed CA files are startup state. Existing legacy/index evidence is preserved, participates in admission/accounting, and cannot be used to reset a fresh capture allowance. The original remediation test explicitly permits stopping or rotating capture at a byte/row quota; stopping preserves evidence without introducing destructive automatic eviction. Actual tests prove quotas stop recording, denied capture leaves target response bytes intact, reserve failure rolls back, saved data remains readable/exportable, and operator cleanup/retry continues. REC-024's original capture-persistence finding is therefore fixed. This does not assert an exclusive OS volume quota, confinement of unrelated processes/stores, or quotas for every separately initiated archive/staging/metadata operation; those broader limitations remain documented rather than being mislabeled as tested capture guarantees. Earlier partial statuses below are historical checkpoints.

The rebuilt Linux/amd64/CGO_ENABLED=0 traffic binary passed 109 tests, zero failures and the same skip (retention-linux-tests.log). ## REC-024: reserve rechecked during native capture/import growth (partial follow-up)

Earlier free-space admission could become stale while SQLite or a body copy was still allocating. All files opened through the traffic VFS now wrap write, truncate and allocation-hint callbacks, including the main database without its own per-file WAL quota. Under the store's native allocation mutex, each logical file extension checks current native volume availability during capture/import, leaving the configured reserve plus 64 KiB for allocation rounding/metadata. Blob writes share that mutex and recheck between bounded chunks; streamed archive blob imports use the same checked writer. Overflow/failed availability checks stop allocation. Record/import hold their existing write lock and activate this reserve boundary through commit/rollback; failed transactions remove new blobs and retain previous evidence. Startup and operator recovery/maintenance keep their separately bounded paths so the margin can fund recovery. This closes in-flight logical growth checks for these ingestion paths, not exclusive OS reservation, unrelated processes/stores, overwrites that cause filesystem extent allocation, or all staging/metadata consumers. REC-024 remains partial for the outstanding retention/aggregate/storage boundaries.

A native regression starts with a saved exchange and an empty WAL, permits the first allocation, then checks the real volume with an impossible reserve on subsequent growth. Capture fails during native commit, leaves only the saved exchange, removes its new body blob, increments the drop count and preserves the target response. Restoring capacity permits another capture and deletion. The parallel import regression requires rollback, blob cleanup and an idempotent successful retry. It exposed an existing accounting defect: ImportArchive returned attempted rows even after commit failed. The function now returns zero for an uncommitted transaction; successful import/current-row/idempotent behavior remains unchanged. A separate writer fixture models reserve loss between chunks and checks the second chunk never reaches storage. These are synthetic reserve-pressure fixtures using actual native SQLite/filesystem operations; no disk was intentionally filled.

The Linux/amd64/CGO_ENABLED=0 traffic binary passed 103 tests, zero failures and the same skip (growth-reserve-linux-tests.log). Windows go test ./server -run '^Test(TrafficCaptureToggle|TaskArchive|Archive|FindingTraffic|ManagerDeleteTaskRestoresFilesAndTraffic|TrafficStorage)' -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s with explicit disposable transport PostgreSQL/config passed 22, zero failures/skips (growth-reserve-server-tests.log, 7.989s). These full/focused checks preceded the import count correction. No other production changes followed.
