## REC-010: original privacy boundary verified and closed

An audit against the retained source records (11, 22, 43, 54 and 92 in the unchanged recovery mapping) distinguishes the reported issue from later hardening work. The original independent producers are captured/restored plaintext bodies and Unix installer credentials, with existing-store repair and independent SQLite/CA checks. The affected principal is an unrelated local reader with a traversable enclosing directory. These sources do not claim protection against an administrator, the trusted owning account rewriting its own files, arbitrary mount/namespace changes or every other platform's pathname races. Those limitations remain documented; they are not silently treated as repaired.

The current-source lifecycle regression starts under a public parent, captures a real synthetic spilled body, exports/imports it, checks legacy evidence, and independently inspects the actual SQLite index and generated CA/private key. Unix requires 0700 owned directories and 0600 body/export/legacy files. Dependency-created Unix SQLite/CA files may use broader individual modes, but their private ancestors are established before creation and an actual different UID cannot read them. Windows checks every owned directory/file's DACL, including dependencies, for only the runtime user, SYSTEM and Administrators. Export files are checked individually under the public parent. Owner reads, body bytes, row counts and the CA/key bytes remain intact.

The test then closes the store, deliberately makes the entire synthetic installation public (including SQLite/CA/blob artifacts), reopens it, and verifies repair without key rotation or evidence loss. The Linux fixture proves successful unrelated-UID reads both before initial protection and after simulated legacy permissions, followed by nine denied reads across new/restored/legacy bodies, SQLite and CA files. This avoids a false pass from already-private Go temporary ancestors. Windows verifies removal of the inherited Everyone grants without creating or impersonating a host account.

Verification: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs ./config ./evidence ./traffic ./server -p 1 -run=^Test.*(Private|Installer|JWT|Archive|StageTaskFiles) -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` passed 57 Windows top-level tests, zero failures/skips, with explicit disposable config/PostgreSQL and hidden installer fixtures (`private-boundary-final-windows-tests.log`). Corresponding current-source Linux binaries passed 58 tests, zero failures/skips, in the existing disposable container with installer and alternate-UID opt-ins (`private-boundary-{privatefs,config,evidence,traffic,server}-linux-tests.log`). Installer fixtures exercise real credential creation/repair in all four new/existing Docker/local branches while stubbing subsequent Docker/Go commands; they are not actual installations. The expanded reopen lifecycle then passed separately on Windows and Linux (`private-boundary-lifecycle-windows-tests.log`, `private-boundary-lifecycle-linux-tests.log`), retaining all alternate-UID outcomes.

REC-010 is fixed for all original independent privacy producers and their existing-data migration requirements. Earlier entries marking it partial describe superseded missing work or separately retained hardening limitations. No general hostile-owner sandbox, privileged-user confidentiality, Darwin runtime verification, actual installer deployment or full application/frontend rerun is claimed. The original failed scan is unchanged. Checkpoint is now 34 fixed and five partial families.

Separately, a real Windows batch fixture with LF and CRLF parameter payloads did not launch a second marker command in the current default CMD mode (`go test ./server -run=^TestCommandTemplateBatchLineBreakCannotLaunchAnotherCommand$ -count=1 -v -timeout=30s`, `batch-linebreak-before-tests.log`). No renderer rejection rule was introduced from that negative result; ambient/nonstandard interpreter behavior remains under REC-003 review.

## REC-010: Windows signing-key publication through pinned handles (partial follow-up)

Windows PublishFile previously closed its exclusive staging handle and then linked and removed the staging file by pathname. Its source and destination could therefore be resolved again after the source handle closed. It now retains the original source and parent handles, creates the source with a private DACL before any write, and publishes using NtSetInformationFile with FILE_LINK_INFORMATION, the pinned RootDirectory and replacement disabled. Cleanup marks the opened staging link for deletion through that same handle rather than deleting whatever occupies its original pathname. The separately published hard link remains intact. Cleanup is idempotent.

The basic FileLinkInformation class is 11, as documented by [Microsoft's enumeration](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/wdm/ne-wdm-_file_information_class) and implemented by the bundled Go runtime. x/sys v0.47.0's constant with that name instead denotes extended class 72; this implementation deliberately uses the basic class. The [native structure](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/ns-ntifs-_file_link_information) supports a destination directory handle and failure when a destination already exists.

The first parent-replacement fixture failed because Windows prohibited renaming the directory while its child was open, even with delete sharing. `publication-before-windows-tests.log` records that OS protection, not a reproduced publication exploit. The corrected test accepts only the observed access/sharing-denied errors and verifies publication/cleanup remain correct while parent replacement is prohibited; on filesystems allowing replacement it checks that publication stays in the original directory. A separate actual source-entry replacement regression renames the live staging file, inserts replacement bytes at its old name and verifies publication retains the original bytes and private ACL, cleanup removes the original staging link and leaves the replacement untouched.

Verification: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs -count=1 -v -timeout=60s` passed 25 Windows tests, zero failures/skips with explicit hidden installer fixtures (`publication-pinned-windows-tests.log`). `go test ./server -run=^TestJWTKey -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` passed nine Windows tests with the disposable config/PostgreSQL, zero failures/skips (`publication-pinned-jwt-windows-tests.log`), including concurrent startup, restart/migration session preservation and hard-linked legacy cleanup. Darwin amd64 privatefs test compilation passed; no Darwin runtime test. Gofmt and git diff --check passed. Linux production code is unchanged from the preceding native verification; full application/frontend suites were not rerun.

This closes the Windows PublishFile pathname publication/cleanup subcase. Other-platform publication, other publication APIs and tree/installer operations, hostile ownership/hard-link trust and namespace/mount boundaries remain under review; REC-010 remains partial. Checkpoint stays 33 fixed and six partial families. No new scan/provider worker, dependency installation, deployment or global OS setting change occurred.

## REC-010: Linux signing-key publication through pinned descriptors (partial follow-up)

The unchanged publication regression failed after its staging parent was renamed and replaced: the original directory received no key, the replacement directory received the replacement source bytes, and cleanup deleted the replacement source. `publication-before-linux-tests.log` preserves that result.

Linux PublishFile now retains its parent descriptor and source inode through writing, sync, exclusive hard-link publication and cleanup. It prefers an unnamed O_TMPFILE inode. Filesystems without that facility use a private exclusive named staging file in the pinned parent. Publication links the live source descriptor into the pinned destination directory, preserving the existing-destination failure required by concurrent JWT startup. Where AT_EMPTY_PATH requires a capability, the implementation uses /proc/self/fd with AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW; it never falls back to a replaceable source pathname. Named fallback cleanup is relative to the pinned parent, and cleanup is idempotent.

Verification: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs -count=1 -v -timeout=60s` with the explicit hidden installer fixtures passed 23 Windows tests, zero failures/skips (`publication-final-windows-tests.log`). The current-source Linux privatefs binary passed 21 tests with installer/read-isolation opt-ins, zero failures/skips (`publication-final-linux-tests.log`). Three selected publication/private-permission/owner-mode tests also passed as nobody. Windows `go test ./server -run=^TestJWTKey -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` passed nine tests and the current-source Linux server binary passed eight, including concurrent startup, migration/session preservation and duplicate cleanup, with zero failures/skips (`publication-jwt-windows-tests.log`, `publication-jwt-linux-tests.log`).

The unprivileged Linux path needs accessible procfs when descriptor linking requires a capability; without it, publication fails closed. O_TMPFILE-unavailable filesystems and older kernels were not runtime-tested. Named fallback cleanup still assumes a caller-owned directory. Windows/other-platform publication, tree walks, installer operations, hostile ownership/hard-link trust and mount/namespace boundaries remain open. This change preserves existing Windows behavior and does not complete REC-010. Checkpoint remains 33 fixed and six partial families. No fresh scan, provider worker, dependency installation or deployment occurred.

## REC-010: private directory creation through pinned parents (partial follow-up)

ProtectDirectory, ProtectTree and ProtectTreePreservingOwner previously called path-based MkdirAll before their native protection step. New unchanged-code regressions show all three APIs create missing directories through a linked parent on both Windows and Linux, even though the later protection returns an error (directory-create-before-windows-tests.log and directory-create-before-linux-tests.log).

Windows/Linux directory protection now traverses and creates components through pinned directory handles/descriptors instead of MkdirAll. Windows uses directory-only NtCreateFile FILE_OPEN_IF with a protected inheritable creation descriptor; existing ancestor ACLs are left intact. The final owned directory is protected through its returned handle. Linux uses O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW openat and mkdirat for missing components, then reopens the directory without following links; newly created owner permissions are repaired before descending if umask removed access. Only the final owned directory and new intermediate directories are protected; existing ancestor modes remain unchanged. Linux literal component/trailing-slash semantics remain, and Windows keeps its previously verified physical/extended/relative path behavior. Darwin/other Unix still uses its prior creation/protection approach. README.md describes the native directory creation boundary.

Regression checks verify linked paths reject before creating any outside directory, all newly created intermediate directories are private, repeated creation is safe, and existing parent permissions stay unchanged. A Windows fixture deliberately gives its existing parent an inheritable Everyone grant: each new nested directory excludes that grant, and the public parent remains untouched. Existing inode/parent replacement, permission-only repair, owner execution and installer tests continue to pass.

Verification: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs -count=1 -v -timeout=60s` with explicit hidden installer fixtures passed 23 top-level Windows tests, zero failures/skips (directory-create-final-windows-tests.log). The current-source Linux privatefs binary passed 20 top-level tests, zero failures/skips, with installer/read-isolation opt-ins; four selected directory/literal-path/owner-mode tests passed as nobody (directory-create-final-linux-tests.log and directory-create-nonroot-linux-tests.log). Current-source config/evidence/traffic/server selections `^Test.*(Private|JWT|Archive|StageTaskFiles)` passed 42 Windows and 43 Linux tests, zero failures/skips, with explicit disposable config/PostgreSQL (directory-create-stores-windows-tests.log and directory-create-stores-linux-tests.log). Darwin amd64 privatefs test compilation passed; no Darwin runtime verification. Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check passed.

Tree enumeration, path-based publication, installer operations, hostile ownership/hard-link trust, mount/namespace boundaries and other-platform ancestor races remain open. In particular, Linux mkdirat does not return an inode handle, so reopening a newly created entry is not a complete hostile-owner race boundary. Full application/frontend suites were not rerun. REC-010 remains partial; checkpoint stays 33 fixed and six partial families. No new scan/provider worker, dependency installation, actual application install/deployment or global OS settings change occurred.

## REC-010: Linux ancestor pinning for native private-file operations (partial follow-up)

The linked-ancestor regression is now shared by Windows/Linux. On the unchanged Linux implementation, all five operations followed a linked parent: read, create, truncate, file protection and directory protection (ancestor-linux-before-tests.log). The strengthened current fixture also verifies unchanged outside 0644 file and 0755 directory permissions, not just an error result.

Linux native private reads, exclusive creation and metadata protection now traverse directories from / through O_PATH|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW descriptors, then open the final component with openat against the pinned parent. Existing-file truncation keeps one parent descriptor across read/write/O_PATH inspection fallbacks, permission repair and writable reacquisition; inode identity checks remain. Literal path components are traversed before lexical cleaning could hide link/../file. Ordinary ./ and non-linked ../ components retain their meaning, directory trailing slashes work, and file trailing slashes reject as wrong type. Directory permissions are never changed by this traversal. Darwin/other Unix retains its previous creation implementation and remains open for ancestor hardening. README.md describes the Linux path behavior.

The pinned-parent replacement test renames the original directory and installs a replacement after opening its descriptor, then reads, protects/truncates and creates against that descriptor. Original objects receive private writes; replacement bytes/modes and parent modes are unchanged. Owner-mode 0444/0200/0000 repair, nonblocking FIFO rejection, archive owner execution preservation and unrelated-reader denial remain covered.

Verification: current-source Linux privatefs binaries passed 18 top-level tests, zero failures/skips, with explicit installer/read-isolation opt-ins and the copied current install.sh fixture (ancestor-final-linux-tests.log). Six selected ancestor/identity/owner-mode tests also passed as the existing nobody user, zero failures/skips (ancestor-nonroot-linux-tests.log). Sequential Linux binaries for config/evidence/traffic/server ran `-test.run=^Test.*(Private|JWT|Archive|StageTaskFiles)` with explicit disposable config/PostgreSQL: 43 passes, zero failures/skips (ancestor-stores-linux-tests.log). The updated shared regression passed on Windows (ancestor-shared-windows-tests.log). GOOS=darwin GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 privatefs test compilation succeeded; Darwin runtime was not tested. Edited Go files are formatted and git diff --check passes.

Directory creation/tree enumeration, path-based publication, installer operations, ownership/hard-link trust, other platform ancestor handling and broader runtime boundaries remain open. This is not a mount/namespace or hostile-owner sandbox. Full application/frontend suites were not rerun. REC-010 remains partial; checkpoint remains 33 fixed and six partial families. No new scan/provider worker, dependency installation, actual application installation/deployment or global OS configuration change occurred.

## REC-010: Windows ancestor pinning for native private-file operations (partial follow-up)

Five unchanged-code regressions reproduced escapes through a linked parent: private read returned outside bytes, exclusive creation produced an outside file, truncation changed outside bytes/permissions, and file/directory permission repair changed outside DACLs (ancestor-before-tests.log). Existing rejection of a final symbolic-link entry did not protect intermediate components.

The Windows native read/create/existing-write/permission-open paths now traverse from a volume/share root one component at a time with NtCreateFile RootDirectory handles. Intermediate directory handles are validated as non-reparse directories; relative component opens use FILE_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT and OBJ_DONT_REPARSE. The final file is opened against the pinned parent, including its private creation descriptor before writing. Parents receive no permission changes. NT status codes are mapped to Win32 errors to preserve existing-file/missing-file retry semantics. Unsupported component/device/stream names and volume roots fail closed. Ordinary, relative, extended local and Unicode paths are verified. UNC/share roots are implemented but have not been runtime-tested; reparse/cloud/mounted directory paths require a physical path rather than silently following them. README.md describes this Windows path requirement.

The first full privatefs run found two integration errors: direct NT write opens lacked metadata-read access, and repeated PathError wrapping prevented os.IsExist from recognizing an existing file. Explicit FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES and one path-error wrapper restore metadata validation and CreateTruncate retries without weakening access/type checks. The intermediate failure is retained in ancestor-focused-tests.log. An installer-fixture invocation then omitted SKOPOS_TEST_BASH and failed without running its stub; the corrected explicit hidden Bash fixture supplies the final installer verification (ancestor-corrected-tests.log retains the setup failure).

The deterministic pinned-parent regression opens the original directory, renames it and installs a replacement, then reads, protects/truncates and creates through the pinned handle. Only the original objects receive private writes; replacement bytes/DACLs and parent permissions remain unchanged. A separate actual NTFS junction fixture also rejects read/create/protection, launched with HideWindow. New-file ACL checks run before writing. `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs -count=1 -v -timeout=60s`, with installer permission opt-in, explicit project install.sh and hidden Git Bash, passed 20 top-level Windows tests, zero failures/skips (ancestor-final-windows-tests.log). `go test ./config ./evidence ./traffic ./server -p 1 -run '^Test.*(Private|JWT|Archive|StageTaskFiles)' -count=1 -v -timeout=90s`, with explicit disposable config/PostgreSQL, passed 42 tests, zero failures/skips (ancestor-stores-windows-tests.log). Edited Go files are formatted and git diff --check passes.

This repairs these Windows native opens, not every tree-walk/publication/installer operation. Ownership, hard-link trust, non-Windows ancestor races and path-based publication remain open. Full application/frontend/Linux/macOS suites were not rerun for this Windows-only change. REC-010 remains partial and the checkpoint remains 33 fixed, six partial families. No new scan/provider worker, dependency installation, actual application install/deployment or global Windows settings change occurred.

## REC-024: progressive decoded-metadata admission (partial follow-up)

A valid manifest with 50,000 tiny exchange objects is only 150,038 raw bytes, but the prior whole-snapshot decoder allocated 70,204,904 bytes before the one-MiB index admission failure. The unchanged-code regression fails its eight-MiB allocation assertion (archive-decoded-before-tests.log), demonstrating collection amplification below the raw-byte ceiling.

The manifest parser now reads exchange/hash arrays entry by entry and admits retained metadata against a separate budget using SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_INDEX_BYTES. Record/hash widths reserve slice-growth headroom; retained strings and actual decoded body capacities are also charged. Charges accumulate across repeated collection fields rather than resetting when an earlier value is replaced. Unknown future values are skipped through syntax-validated tokens without constructing their maps/arrays. Numeric tokens retain their representation so an ignored future number does not have to fit float64. Case-insensitive fields, normal duplicate-field last-wins/null behavior, base64 bodies and existing schema validation are preserved within capacity. README.md describes the decoded-metadata ceiling and the need to raise capacity/restart for legitimate collections that exceed it. No configured cap changed.

The first progressive-parser run exposed a real classification issue in an existing growth boundary test: json.Decoder.More discards a peek error, and the next token read could report EOF after consuming the excess-byte probe. The bounded reader now retains terminal errors across subsequent reads, preserving the localized budget failure instead of hiding it as EOF. That unchanged boundary assertion passes; archive-decoded-focused-tests.log retains the failed intermediate result and archive-decoded-corrected-tests.log records the correction. The amplification fixture now allocates approximately 1.6 MiB before rejecting, without any row/blob changes. Format compatibility fixtures compare the progressive result with the prior json.Unmarshal behavior for valid and invalid inputs.

Verification: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=120s` passed 88 top-level Windows tests, zero failures, one opt-in TestProxyClientTransportAdmission skip (archive-decoded-final-windows-tests.log). The complete current-source Linux traffic binary passed 76 top-level tests, zero failures, the same opt-in skip (archive-decoded-final-linux-tests.log). `go test ./server -run '^Test(TaskArchive|FindingTrafficArchive|Archive)' -count=1 -v -timeout=90s`, with explicit disposable config/PostgreSQL, passed ten Windows tests, zero failures/skips (archive-decoded-server-tests.log). Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check passed. Full application/frontend and the opt-in proxy transport suite were not rerun.

The snapshot remains materialized within metadata admission; per-record decoding/JSON buffers and runtime overhead mean this is not a hard total-process heap limit. Hard per-transaction WAL limits, extraction/other staging, SQLite temporary/legacy storage and aggregate disk consumption remain open. REC-024 remains partial; overall checkpoint stays 33 fixed and six partial families. No new scan/provider request, scan worker, installation or deployment occurred.

## REC-024: bounded traffic manifest reads (partial follow-up)

A synthetic version-1 manifest padded with eight MiB of whitespace was accepted under a one-MiB configured index limit and allocated 8,419,408 bytes before admission. The unchanged-code regression failed on both acceptance and allocation assertions (archive-manifest-before-tests.log). ImportArchive now checks the opened regular manifest's size against SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_INDEX_BYTES before decoding. A capped reader separately enforces that same limit during decoding and trailing-whitespace validation, including a source that grows after metadata inspection. One probe byte detects excess input without passing it to the decoder or overflowing a limit+1 calculation. Decoding requires exactly one JSON value, retaining Unmarshal's trailing-data rejection, duplicate-field last-wins behavior, and unknown-field compatibility.

The oversize fixture now rejects with the existing localized index-budget error before any row/blob mutation; its focused run allocated 4,064 bytes. A corrected manifest is immediately retryable. Exact-size and whitespace boundaries, growth in the body/trailing whitespace, invalid input, second objects/scalars, duplicate fields, and unknown fields pass regression checks. README.md explains the manifest byte ceiling and that an operator may need to raise the configured index limit/restart for legitimate large metadata, including retries. No configured capacity was changed.

Verification: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=120s` passed 86 top-level Windows tests, zero failures, one opt-in TestProxyClientTransportAdmission skip (archive-manifest-final-windows-tests.log). The complete current-source Linux traffic binary passed 74 top-level tests, zero failures, the same one opt-in skip (archive-manifest-final-linux-tests.log). `go test ./server -run '^Test(TaskArchive|FindingTrafficArchive|Archive)' -count=1 -v -timeout=90s`, with explicit disposable config/PostgreSQL, passed ten Windows tests with zero failures/skips (archive-manifest-server-tests.log). Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check passed. Full application/frontend and the opt-in proxy transport suite were not rerun.

This bounds raw manifest input, not aggregate decoded-object/process memory: import still materializes the bounded snapshot, and JSON fields can amplify allocation. Streaming decoded collections, hard per-transaction WAL limits, extraction/other staging, SQLite temporary/legacy files and aggregate disk consumption remain open. REC-024 remains partial; overall checkpoint stays 33 fixed and six partial families. No new scan/provider request, scan worker, install or deployment occurred.

## REC-024: bounded streaming archive blob imports (partial follow-up)

The importer previously read each complete archive blob before storage admission, including blobs that could not fit and deduplicated retries. A synthetic valid archive containing a 64 MiB zero body reproduced approximately 67.14 MB of allocation per import in all four cases (one-byte budget rejection twice, exact-capacity import and idempotent retry; archive-blob-before-tests.log). This measures the local importer, not a live attack or general process-memory ceiling.

Archive blobs now use one opened source for size admission and streaming checksum verification. New bodies are admitted before body reads; the private temporary output cannot receive more bytes than the admitted source size. Growth, truncation and checksum mismatches reject publication. Successful files are synced and published at the content-addressed path, and only then charged against blob usage. Existing deduplicated blobs require no new capacity but still verify the incoming checksum through a bounded buffer. The archive's existing SQLite transaction and rollback cleanup retain atomic row/blob behavior. No configured cap was increased and no evidence was evicted. Source extraction/staging and the JSON manifest are not bounded by this change.

Observed Windows allocations for the same 64 MiB fixture were 16,048 and 15,776 bytes for rejected imports, 55,520 bytes for the admitted import, and 46,064 bytes for its retry. Regression coverage also checks growing/truncated/corrupt sources, no temporary write beyond admitted size, corruption cleanup, successful retry, existing hot-row precedence, and budget rollback/restart. `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=120s` passed 84 top-level tests on Windows, zero failures, one opt-in TestProxyClientTransportAdmission skip (archive-blob-final-windows-tests.log). The corruption retry assertion was subsequently tightened to require a nil error; its focused Windows rerun passed. The complete current-source Linux traffic binary passed 72 top-level tests, zero failures, the same one opt-in skip (archive-blob-final-linux-tests.log). `go test ./server -run '^Test(TaskArchive|FindingTrafficArchive|Archive)' -count=1 -v -timeout=90s`, with explicit disposable configuration/PostgreSQL, passed ten top-level Windows tests, zero failures/skips (archive-blob-server-tests.log). Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check passed.

REC-024 remains partial: hard per-transaction WAL limits, JSON/extraction and other staging, SQLite temporary files, legacy storage and aggregate disk consumption still require work. Full application/frontend suites and the opt-in proxy transport suite were not rerun for this import change. Overall checkpoint remains 33 fixed and six partial families. No new scan, provider call, scan worker, installation or deployment occurred.

## REC-004 / REC-034: runtime executable selection requires human approval (partial follow-up)

Current static subject construction previously returned no operation for variable/command-substitution executable names, including wrapped names and literal nested programs. With no rule match and the fallback judge disabled, the production guard allowed these calls. Recognized static operations now retain a separate uncertainty flag for their executable position; static argument data does not set it. Supported wrapper unwrapping preserves each word's known/unknown provenance rather than treating the literal string <dynamic> as a runtime value.

After evaluating ordinary raw/semantic rule matches in their existing priority order, an unresolved executable and any active restrictive tool-input rule produce an ask decision. This platform decision bypasses the LLM fallback judge, carries the original input and current policy digest into the existing approval audit, and has a fixed five-minute deny-on-timeout. An explicit matching allow still takes precedence. Cancellation/missing approval blocks at the existing hook; disabling tool interception or removing restrictive input rules retains the configured unrestricted behavior. English/Korean reasons and both READMEs describe the changed workflow.

Verification: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./intercept ./guard ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` with explicit disposable config/PostgreSQL passed 46 Windows tests, zero failures/skips (`runtime-executable-final-windows-tests.log`). Current-source Linux binaries passed 32 interceptor, three guard and 11 locale tests, zero failures/skips (`runtime-executable-{intercept,guard,locale}-linux-tests.log`). Unit coverage includes 32 dynamic-head tool/executor combinations, raw allow precedence, static argument data, nonexecution tools, empty/allow-only policies and Korean text. Eighteen real-hook/disposable-PostgreSQL cases per platform cover Bash, shell_open and shell_send in both languages: human approval, human denial and context expiry without a decision. Their dispatcher sink is deliberately a nonexecuting stub. They preserve the original input/audit and verify that an always-allow synthetic reviewer is never called, even with permissive judge timeout settings. No OS containment is inferred from the stub.

`go test ./server -run=^TestCustom(OperationsReviewResolvedPlansThroughSDK|ConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule)$ -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` passed two Windows tests with explicit disposable SDK config/PostgreSQL/Python, zero failures/skips (`runtime-executable-sdk-windows-tests.log`). This preserves all 48 existing bilingual synthetic-provider custom command/script/HTTP approval cases and final executor-rule enforcement. A fault-injection overlay disabling only the new ask fallback fails the new runtime-executable tests (`runtime-executable-negative-overlay.json`, `runtime-executable-negative-tests.log`); it is not an unchanged-HEAD baseline. The first test-only baseline also exposed an invalid nil-cache fixture, corrected to a loaded empty cache rather than changing product failure handling. Gofmt and git diff --check passed. Full application/frontend suites were not rerun.

This repairs runtime-selected executable heads through supported static wrappers/nested programs. Dynamic arguments to a fixed executable, unknown shell-program input, unsupported wrappers, external configuration/scripts, incomplete interactive fragments and OS/runtime confinement still require separate review. REC-004/034 remain partial; checkpoint stays 34 fixed and five partial families. No new scan/provider worker, dependency installation, deployment or global OS setting change occurred.

## REC-004 / REC-034: literal pipeline input (partial follow-up)

Unchanged-code policy regressions confirm that literal printf/echo/cat output piped into a shell escaped restrictive semantic rules. The baseline covered ten pipeline forms, both deny/ask actions and Bash/shell_send (`literal-pipe-before-tests.log`). Plain data pipelines and explicit input overrides passed their baseline preservation checks.

Static operation analysis now propagates known literal pipeline output into stdin-reading shells, including single-command blocks/subshells, simple cat passthrough chains and supported invocation wrappers. It reconstructs unambiguous echo data and printf formats limited to %s, %% and supported literal escapes. Explicit stdin redirects override the pipe; script files, syntax-only invocations, data consumers and redirected producer stdout do not become executable subjects. A group's stdout redirection cannot hide a program executed by an internal pipeline, because pipe creation replaces the producer's inherited stdout while preserving inherited stdin. Existing raw allow precedence, audit inputs and execution arguments are retained.

Producer evaluation is cached per AST statement. A 512-cat-chain regression verifies long static pipelines remain covered. Reconstructed printf input/output is bounded at 1 MiB; unsupported format widths and oversized repeated output remain unresolved instead of allocating unbounded generated strings. No shell process, filesystem read, variable expansion or environment lookup runs during reconstruction.

Verification: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./intercept ./guard -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` with explicit disposable config/PostgreSQL passed 32 top-level Windows tests, zero failures/skips (`literal-pipe-final-windows-tests.log`). The current-source Linux interceptor and guard binaries passed 30 and two tests respectively, zero failures/skips (`literal-pipe-intercept-linux-tests.log`, `literal-pipe-guard-linux-tests.log`). The real seeded-rule guard covers six new pipeline inputs through both Bash and shell_send without executing their destructive/network payloads. Actual hidden Git Bash and existing-container Bash separately passed 36 harmless echo-only pipeline fixtures, including execution/data/noexec/stdin-override/output-redirection cases (`literal-pipe-runtime-probes.py` and `.json`). The first Windows runtime probe using env -i without PATH could not locate bash; the retained probe explicitly supplies PATH=/usr/bin:/bin. Gofmt and git diff --check passed; full application/frontend suites were not rerun.

This closes the supported literal-pipeline subcase. Expanded/generated producers, multi-command shared streams, other transformations/format modes, external configuration, unsupported wrappers, incomplete interactive fragments and runtime isolation remain under review. REC-004/034 remain partial; overall checkpoint stays 33 fixed and six partial families. No new scan/provider worker, dependency installation, deployment or OS setting change occurred.

## REC-004 / REC-034: inherited single-command group input (partial follow-up)

Four unchanged-code policy regressions reproduced bypasses when a literal stdin program belongs to an enclosing brace group or subshell instead of the shell command itself. Statement traversal now forwards explicit input redirections through single-command groups, including nested wrapper/group chains. Inner redirects are applied after inherited redirects, preserving input replacement and fd/output semantics. Shared streams after multiple commands remain unresolved rather than assuming that already-consumed bytes are executable. Cat input, syntax checking, and an inner /dev/null or replacement here-string remain data/nonexecution controls.

The first runtime probe contradicted a newly added negative-test assumption: `{ bash & } <<EOF` executes the explicitly supplied body on both tested Bash platforms. The incorrect negative expectation was replaced with a positive bypass regression, and the parser also forwards this group's literal input to its asynchronous child. This correction follows observed shell behavior; no production assertion or acceptance criterion was relaxed.

Verification on current source: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./intercept ./guard -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=60s`, with explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG and SKOPOS_PG_DSN, passed 30 top-level tests on Windows, zero failures/skips (shell-group-stdin-final-windows-tests.log). Current-source Linux binaries for both packages passed sequentially in the existing PostgreSQL test container: 30 top-level tests, zero failures/skips (shell-group-stdin-final-linux-tests.log). The seeded guard tests both Bash and shell_send with eight additional grouped destructive/HTTP-method command texts; those payloads are reviewed, never executed. The retained `shell-group-stdin-probe.py` ran ten echo-only fixtures on hidden Git Bash and ten on container Bash, all passing (shell-group-stdin-runtime.log). Edited Go files are formatted and git diff --check passes. Full application/frontend suites were not rerun for this parser change.

REC-004 and REC-034 remain partial: multi-command shared streams, pipelines, generated/expanded programs, configuration files, unsupported wrappers and runtime isolation remain open. The overall checkpoint remains 33 fixed and six partial families. No new scan, provider call or scan worker was started.

## REC-004 / REC-034: literal shell stdin (partial follow-up)

Five new unchanged-code regressions reproduced restricted-command bypasses through literal here-documents and here-strings, including wrapper invocation, Bash named options, and tab-stripping here-documents. These tests only submit command text to policy; they never execute the destructive commands. The parser now inspects statement redirections and distinguishes executable shell stdin from command strings, script filenames, positional data, and syntax-only invocation. Only the final direct fd-0 input redirect supplies the nested program. Quoted literal bodies are parsed without expansion; dynamic bodies, input files, and descriptor copies remain unresolved. Output redirects and other descriptors do not become programs. Existing command-substitution traversal, deny/ask decisions, and explicit raw operator overrides remain intact.

Actual verification: with GOTOOLCHAIN=local, explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG and SKOPOS_PG_DSN, `go test ./intercept ./guard -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=60s` passed on Windows (29 top-level tests, zero failures/skips; shell-stdin-windows-tests.log). The seeded PostgreSQL guard exercises both Bash and shell_send, including six additional literal stdin commands. The current-source Linux cross-compiled interceptor suite passed in the existing test container (27 top-level tests, zero failures/skips; shell-stdin-linux-tests.log). Eight harmless echo-only Git Bash probes passed with CREATE_NO_WINDOW, checking execution, -s positional data, named options, tab stripping, noexec, -c precedence, and later stdin replacement. The first clean-environment probe could not locate bash after env -i cleared PATH; the corrected fixture explicitly supplies /usr/bin:/bin. No application configuration or system PATH was changed. Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check passed.

REC-004 and REC-034 remain partial: inherited group/pipeline input, generated or expanded programs, shell configuration, unsupported wrappers, and runtime execution boundaries still require repair. Full application/frontend suites and Linux guard execution were not rerun for this change. No new security scan, provider request, or scan worker was started.

## REC-003: Windows batch percent expansion (partial follow-up)

An actual fixed `.cmd` fixture that never reads its arguments reproduced caller-side command injection: a parameter containing `%SW_BATCH_BREAK%`, with that environment variable containing a quote, launched a second harmless echo outside the batch file. The unchanged-renderer regression failed (5.766s; batch-percent-before-tests.log). This agrees with the documented environment-expansion boundary in the primary [BatBadBut research](https://flatt.tech/research/posts/batbadbut-you-cant-securely-execute-commands-on-windows/); the local reproduction, rather than the advisory alone, establishes this subcase.

Resolved `.cmd`/`.bat` targets now escape percent signs with the CMD-specific `%%cd:~,%` representation and omit the terminal-backslash doubling used for conventional native argv parsers. The first percent-escaping change stopped the extra command but altered a trailing backslash; the exact-data regression retained that failure (6.791s; batch-percent-roundtrip-tests.log). The corrected batch branch passes `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^TestCommandTemplateBatch' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s` with explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN and Python fixture (two passes, zero skips, 11.707s; batch-final-focused-tests.log). The boundary fixture checks quote/backslash and environment/CMDCMDLINE quote-expansion payloads. The benign-data fixture checks exact percent-bearing strings, literal variable-looking text, spaces and a trailing backslash through the batch parameter and an environment data channel.

Conventional native literal/runtime-expanded arguments, static alias pinning and managed PowerShell data also pass `go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplateNativeExternalArguments|CommandTemplateNativeRuntimeExpansion|CommandTemplateStaticAliasTargetPinned|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip)$' -count=1 -v -timeout=60s` with the same explicit fixture environment (four top-level passes, zero failures/skips, 56.080s; batch-native-regression-tests.log). Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check pass. Full application/frontend/Linux and the entire renderer selections were not rerun for this batch branch.

This verifies the local default CMD mode and these fixtures only. Ambient delayed expansion/disabled command extensions, newlines, explicit `cmd.exe /c` interpreted program data, arbitrary batch-body interpolation and other nonstandard native parsers remain open. No global CMD registry/mode settings were changed, and REC-003 remains partial. No new scan or provider worker was started.

## REC-003: static alias rebinding during argument evaluation (partial follow-up)

Actual execution reproduced an additional state mismatch: an argument rebinding a static alias from Python to Write-Output switched the invocation to the managed target after native quoting had already been selected. Output included the Python option/script strings and native-encoded empty quotes instead of the intended native argv result. Unchanged-renderer regression `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^TestCommandTemplateStaticAliasTargetPinned$' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s` fails (2.532s; static-alias-before-tests.log). This is not just a quote-loss hypothesis; the observed executable behavior changes.

Commands containing eligible placeholder arguments now capture static names as well as dynamic heads before argument evaluation. Bare static invocation adds the call operator; existing '&'/dot operators are preserved. All encoded arguments use that invocation's captured command reference, removing per-argument name re-resolution. Selected static names remain quoted literal text in the reviewed script rather than being hidden as Base64. Argument values keep their existing data representation. Empty/inconsistent parser references fail closed. The focused bare/explicit-call static alias regression passes after capture (3.700s; static-alias-focused-tests.log).

This deliberately defines selection at invocation start: argument-side alias rebinding no longer retargets that same invocation. Its side effect remains available to later calls. The extended regression verifies both pinned Python argv and the next alias call using Write-Output with exact managed string data; `go test ./server -run '^TestCommandTemplateStaticAliasTargetPinned$' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s` passes (3.876s; static-alias-next-call-tests.log). The native/managed transition is now explicit behavior rather than a claim that late alias mutation has identical shell semantics.

Passed full current-renderer selection: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|Custom(ConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule|.*Operation|.*Redirect.*|.*Resolved.*)|RenderTemplate)' -count=1 -v -timeout=180s`, with explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN and Python fixture. Twenty-one top-level passes, zero failures/skips, 165.305s; static-alias-final-tests.log. This includes 42 dynamic cases, 24 nested cases, all earlier native literal/runtime/task/alias/parameterized-path and managed function/PowerShell/Bash/rejection/cancellation/one-pass checks, plus 48 bilingual synthetic-provider SDK approval cases and configured-rule/redirect regressions. The subsequent test-only next-call assertion is verified separately above; production renderer code did not change. Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check pass. Full application/frontend/Linux suites were not rerun for this Windows renderer change. REC-003 remains partial for nonstandard native argument-parser review; no general shell or network sandbox claim is made. No new paid scan/provider request, dependency installation, deployment or global OS alias/account change occurred; checkpoint remains 33 fixed and 6 open.

## REC-003: dynamic executable expressions (partial follow-up)

Actual execution reproduced the remaining variable-command gap: `& $application` selected Python, but its parameter was rendered with managed quoting and reached the process as two argv entries. The unchanged-renderer regression `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^TestCommandTemplateDynamicExecutable$/^0$/^0$/^0$' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s` fails (3.092s; dynamic-executable-before-tests.log). No target operation, provider or destructive command was used.

For eligible arguments with a nonliteral command head, the parser now records that head's source span and a fresh internal binding name. Rendering captures the command expression once in caller scope before argument evaluation, resolving a string to CommandInfo while preserving existing command objects and scriptblocks. Argument encoding uses this captured selection, so argument-side mutations of the executable variable cannot switch its native/managed classification. Existing '&' and dot invocation semantics are retained. Native encoding checks CommandInfo type before inspecting CommandType; managed scriptblocks retain their original string values. Literal command paths and existing static commands keep their previous handling. Parsing still evaluates only inert markers, not operator command expressions or parameter values.

The first focused run passes 24 dynamic native cases plus '&'/dot scriptblock scope checks (23.731s; dynamic-executable-focused-tests.log). The expanded broad selection `go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|Custom(ConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule|.*Operation|.*Redirect.*|.*Resolved.*)|RenderTemplate)' -count=1 -v -timeout=180s` finishes with 18 top-level passes, 1 failure and no skips (130.761s; dynamic-executable-final-tests.log). The sole failure was a new fixture collision: after adding an executable placeholder named exe, the existing operator variable `${exe}` became a requested placeholder in a prohibited expansion context. The renderer correctly rejected it. The fixture now uses a distinct operator variable application and explicitly asserts that the original expansion-context collision stays rejected; no expected native value or evaluation count changed. Production renderer code did not change after this broad run.

Passed corrected current-source selection: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplateDynamicExecutable|CommandTemplateParameterizedExecutable|CustomOperationsReviewResolvedPlansThroughSDK)$' -count=1 -v -timeout=90s`, with explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN and Python selection. Three top-level passes, zero skips/failures, 52.540s; dynamic-executable-corrected-final-tests.log. Forty-two actual native cases cover variables, properties, subexpressions, expandable names, CommandInfo values, parenthesized literal paths and mixed expandable placeholder paths. They preserve exact argument bytes for quotes/backslashes, empty and Unicode/shell-looking data, keep command and argument side effects at one evaluation, and preserve the selected executable when an argument changes its variable. Managed '&'/dot scriptblocks retain their original caller-scope effects. The twelve parameterized path cases and 48 bilingual synthetic-provider SDK approval cases pass again. The unchanged renderer's other native/nested/alias/managed/Bash/rejection/cancellation/redirect/configured-rule cases passed in the broad run above. Separate `go test ./server -run '^TestCommandTemplateDynamicAliasTargetPinned$' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s` passes two real alias-name/alias-object rebinding cases (2.669s; dynamic-alias-target-tests.log). The selected native alias target remains pinned while an argument replaces the alias with Write-Output; no extra fix was needed for this check.

Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check pass. English/Korean documentation reflects supported dynamic command expressions. Full application/frontend/Linux suites were not rerun, and the broad run's fixture failure is retained rather than presented as a clean run. REC-003 still includes compatibility/security review for nonstandard native argument parsers; this is not a runtime sandbox or a full-family closure. No new paid scan/provider request, dependency installation, deployment or global OS alias/account change occurred; checkpoint remains 33 fixed and 6 open.

## REC-003: nested literal parameters (partial follow-up)

Actual native execution reproduced the unresolved mixed-nesting failure: a quoted argument combining a literal parameter inside a PowerShell subexpression with another parameter outside it reached Python as several argv entries, with changed bytes. Unchanged-renderer regression: `go test ./server -run '^TestCommandTemplateNativeNestedParameters$' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s`, 2.091s; nested-native-before-tests.log. The initial repeated-argument-text collision hypothesis was ruled out by the existing unique per-occurrence marker design and is not a reported vulnerability.

The parser now records UTF-8 byte spans for eligible command arguments, separately from each marker's quote context. The renderer composes nested arguments inside their containing expression, applies each literal parameter in its own context once, then encodes the completed native string. Collection also covers arguments with placeholders only inside subexpressions. Bounds/source checks reject inconsistent parser spans; non-ASCII/non-BMP operator text retains correct offsets. An intermediate splice passed the simple nested cases but failed the added native-within-native case, changing quotes/backslashes (67.099s broad run; nested-native-final-tests.log). Its isolated generated-source failure is retained in nested-native-diagnostic-tests.log. Literal native argument values are now represented as Base64-decoded UTF-8 data expressions, and the native encoder constructs its quote character numerically rather than embedding double-quote tokens inside another expandable subexpression. These are data expressions, not an extra evaluation of operator code. The related PowerShell project [nested-quoting issue](https://github.com/PowerShell/PowerShell/issues/17887) informed this representation; it does not independently prove the exact local failure's cause. The previously failing isolated native-within-native case then passed (2.657s; nested-native-serialized-focused-tests.log). No assertion or expected argument value was relaxed.

Passed final current-source command: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|Custom(ConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule|.*Operation|.*Redirect.*|.*Resolved.*)|RenderTemplate)' -count=1 -v -timeout=120s`, with explicitly selected disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN and Python fixture. Seventeen top-level passes, zero skips/failures, 88.946s; nested-native-serialized-final-tests.log. Twenty-four nested cases exercise literal subexpressions, managed and native inner commands, side-effect counting, parameters both inside/outside or only inside the expression, empty values, hostile quote/backslash/shell-looking strings, Unicode and non-BMP operator text. Every native result retains one exact argument; counted subexpressions execute once. Earlier native literal/runtime/alias transitions, managed functions, PowerShell/Bash quoting, rejection/cancellation, one-pass replacement and 48 bilingual synthetic-provider SDK approval cases remain green; redirect/configured-rule checks pass too. ## REC-003: aliases becoming native applications (partial follow-up)

Parsing-time executable classification missed commands rebound by earlier template statements. An actual SDK executor regression changes the initially managed Write-Output name to the explicitly selected Python executable; one literal parameter containing backslash/quote/space arrives as two argv entries. The unchanged renderer fails the new regression (2.024s; native-alias-application-before-tests.log). This is actual argument-boundary failure, not destructive execution or a mock binder result.

The inert PowerShell AST parser now identifies eligible arguments of every static command name, rather than only applications known in the parser process. At execution time the existing encoder resolves the command in caller scope before evaluating the argument expression: native applications receive legacy native quoting, while cmdlets/functions retain ordinary managed strings. This also supports a previously unknown alias created earlier by the template. Parser-side Get-Command and its function-name exclusion are no longer needed; operator code still executes only through the reviewed SDK. Dynamic executable expressions and nested-marker arguments remain outside this repaired path.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|Custom(ConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule|.*Operation|.*Redirect.*|.*Resolved.*)|RenderTemplate)' -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` with explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN and SKOPOS_TEST_PYTHON: 15 top-level passes, zero skips, 55.795s; native-alias-application-final-tests.log. Eighteen new actual native argv cases cover managed/unknown names, bare/single-quoted/runtime-expanded parameters, empty values, quotes/backslashes and Unicode. The earlier 48 native literal/runtime cases, caller/task PATH/directory/environment/pipeline checks, native-to-managed aliases, managed PowerShell/Bash/rejection/cancellation and 48 bilingual synthetic-provider SDK approval cases also pass; redirect and configured-rule controls remain green.

## Windows compatibility: skill API path round-trip

The remaining historical Windows full-server failure was a production path-format mismatch. skillRelPath returned native backslashes, and walkSkillFiles emitted backslashes inside nested directory/file names. The skill editor splits API paths on '/', so this also prevented a consistent tree/list/read workflow. API outputs now normalize to forward slashes; filesystem operations still use filepath.Join. Character whitelists, traversal rejection, native absolute-path checks and upload validation are unchanged. No test expectation was relaxed.

Directory/read/delete response fields remain canonical too.

## REC-010: permission repair through pinned objects (partial follow-up)

Source inspection found that ordinary and owner-mode permission repair still used named SetNamedSecurityInfo/os.Chmod after separate path/type checks. A replacement between those operations could redirect permission changes. Windows and Linux repair now open the final entry without following it, check file/directory role through the opened object and apply permissions to that object. Windows uses a metadata/WRITE_DAC handle with OPEN_REPARSE_POINT and BACKUP_SEMANTICS, retaining delete sharing, and SetSecurityInfo applies the corresponding file/directory DACL. Linux uses O_PATH/NOFOLLOW and descriptor-based chmod; owner-preserving repair derives 0700 owner bits from the opened inode rather than the earlier Lstat result. Directory inheritance, already-private read-only objects, legacy mode repair and file contents retain their intended behavior. Other operating systems retain their earlier path-based repair implementation.

Native regressions reject links to files/directories and wrong role types without changing outside bytes/permissions. A deterministic renamed-file fixture pins the original metadata object, places an unrelated replacement at its former name and verifies that only the original object's permissions change; original/replacement bytes and parent grants remain intact. These are exact final-entry/handle checks, not an exploit run against the old race or proof of safe ancestor traversal.

An added Windows compatibility fixture initially denied metadata as well as data access (permission-handles-restricted-acl-before-tests.log and subsequent focused/corrected logs). ProtectFile's existing preliminary Lstat needs metadata access, so that fixture demanded more than the intended no-data-access condition. It now grants metadata/synchronization and security repair only, explicitly verifies that os.ReadFile fails before repair, then verifies private ACLs and exact credential bytes afterward. Production open flags returned to the original tested metadata flags; the final change does not bypass the type check or grant data access before repair. The corrected requirement is recorded rather than disguising those failed fixture runs as successful verification.

Final Windows `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs -count=1 -v -timeout=45s` with hidden installer opt-ins passes 16 top-level cases, zero failures/skips, 5.343s (permission-handles-final-windows-tests.log). The affected-store selection `go test ./config ./evidence ./traffic ./server -p 1 -run '^Test.*(Private|JWT|Archive|StageTaskFiles)' -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` with explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN passes 35 top-level cases, zero failures/skips (permission-handles-stores-windows-tests.log). Config credential reads, evidence publication/read repair, traffic archives, JWT/session migration/concurrency, archive journal recovery and API behavior remain covered. Production flags/code for this store run match the final Windows implementation; the added ACL fixture was verified in the final complete privatefs run.

Current Linux test binaries cross-compiled with GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 pass in the existing disposable container: complete privatefs suite 15 cases with installer/alternate-reader opt-ins, affected config/evidence/traffic/server selection 36 cases, zero failures/skips (permission-handles-linux-tests.log, permission-handles-stores-linux-tests.log). Four native permission/owner-mode/archive-mode cases also pass under the existing nobody account (permission-handles-nonroot-linux-tests.log), including owner-readable/write-only/mode-000 repair and executable/read-only archive payloads. The privatefs binary cross-compiles for darwin/amd64; no macOS runtime or older-kernel/procfs-absence test was run. Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check pass; full application/frontend suites were not rerun.

REC-010 remains partial for ancestor replacement/ownership, traversal/publication and other independent races, including permission repair on other operating systems. Pinning final entries does not constrain mutable ancestors or hard-linked aliases. Checkpoint remains 33 fixed and 6 open. No new scan/provider worker, dependency, deployment or global OS/account change occurred.

## REC-010: pinned protection and truncation (partial follow-up)

Current-source inspection found a distinct write-side replacement window in CreateTruncate: after exclusive creation returned already-exists, ProtectFile repaired permissions by pathname and a separate os.OpenFile with O_TRUNC reopened that pathname. The protection and truncation therefore could address different file objects. This turn did not run an exploit against the old race; the evidence is the separate source operations and a deterministic opened-object regression for the replacement boundary.

CreateTruncate now opens existing files without truncation, validates regular-file type, repairs permissions through the opened handle and calls Truncate on that same handle. Windows uses OPEN_EXISTING/OPEN_REPARSE_POINT, rejects directory/reparse attributes and requests WRITE_DAC; SetSecurityInfo applies the private DACL to the handle rather than its name. Delete sharing retains rename/retirement compatibility. Linux uses no-follow/nonblocking inspection, protects the inspected inode, then verifies writable-handle identity before truncation. The inspection handle remains live through the identity check. Readable, write-only and mode-000 owner files remain repairable: mode-000 inspection uses O_PATH and descriptor-based fchmodat with AT_EMPTY_PATH, falling back to the live descriptor's /proc/self/fd path on kernels lacking that operation. Missing procfs on those older kernels fails closed; that fallback environment was not reproduced.

The new deterministic regression opens a legacy journal, renames it, installs an unrelated replacement at its former pathname, then protects/truncates/writes through the original handle. The original inode receives private replacement bytes; the unrelated entry retains its bytes and permissions. A direct symbolic-link destination is rejected without modifying its target, and parent grants remain unchanged. Linux owner-mode cases exercise 0444, 0200 and 0000 through the actual CreateTruncate API. Other platforms retain preliminary path-based permission repair to preserve existing owner-mode workflows, then check the opened identity before truncation; their permission-repair races remain open.

Passed Windows commands: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs -count=1 -v -timeout=45s`, enabling the existing hidden installer opt-ins, has 13 top-level passes, no failures/skips, 4.910s (private-truncate-windows-tests.log). `go test ./server -run '^Test(Archive|TaskArchive|StageTaskFiles)' -count=1 -v -timeout=60s` with explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN has 11 top-level passes, no failures/skips, 2.424s (private-truncate-archive-windows-tests.log). Archive payload/journal round trips, interrupted move/install recovery, rollback, traversal/link rejection and API queue/list behavior remain covered.

Current-source Linux privatefs/server test binaries cross-compiled with GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 pass in the existing disposable container: complete privatefs suite 13 top-level passes with installer/alternate-reader opt-ins, and selected archive suite 14 top-level passes, no failures/skips (private-truncate-linux-tests.log, private-truncate-archive-linux-tests.log). The same Linux binary's `TestPrivateTruncateOwnerModes` also passes under the existing nobody account, proving mode repair without root access (private-truncate-nonroot-linux-tests.log). No system account or shared ancestor permissions were changed. The privatefs test binary cross-compiles for darwin/amd64; macOS runtime and older Linux kernels were not tested. Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check pass; full application/frontend suites were not rerun.

REC-010 remains partial for ancestor/ownership races, path-based tree/permission repair, publication and other independent boundaries. Handle pinning does not make shared writable ancestors safe or prevent permitted concurrent content writes/hard-link aliases. Checkpoint remains 33 fixed and 6 open. No new scan/provider workers, dependencies, deployment or global OS changes occurred.

## REC-010: pinned private-file reads (partial follow-up)

An unchanged-reader regression reproduced a final-entry gap: privatefs.ReadFile followed a symbolic link and returned the unrelated target's bytes. `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs -run '^TestPrivateReadRejectsIndirectFiles$' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s` fails on Windows (0.434s; private-read-before-tests.log). This is direct helper-boundary evidence, not a claim that all existing callers accept arbitrary paths; the JWT loader already performs a separate ProtectFile check, leaving a replacement window between that check and its later read.

Private reads now reject nonregular/indirect entries and validate the opened object's identity before reading bytes. Windows opens the reparse point itself, rejects reparse/directory attributes, and obtains an eager file-ID snapshot with handle-based Stat. It keeps that snapshot handle open through the checked read, retaining read/write/delete sharing needed by concurrent JWT cleanup. Linux uses O_NOFOLLOW/O_CLOEXEC/O_NONBLOCK, captures an opened inode and retains its snapshot handle through the read; a swapped FIFO cannot block before type validation. Other platforms retain Lstat/open identity validation with their ordinary open behavior; native no-follow/nonblocking enforcement was not implemented or tested there.

The first implementation incorrectly trusted Windows path-based Lstat identity: Go 1.26.9's os.SameFile resolves that identity lazily via loadFileId, after replacement. The deterministic rename/replacement test therefore returned the replacement bytes (private-read-final-windows-tests.log, 5.071s). Source inspection of the installed Go os/types_windows.go confirmed the lazy lookup. The corrected snapshot uses handle-based Stat with an already captured ID; the test keeps its original requirement and verifies both replacement rejection before data and continued reading of the pinned retired object. Windows and Linux snapshot handles also keep the inspected file object alive while its name is retired. Tests cover file-entry replacement, not every ancestor or permissions race.

Passed current Windows suite: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs -count=1 -v -timeout=45s`, explicitly enabling the existing hidden Git Bash installer permission fixtures: 12 top-level passes, zero failures/skips, 4.922s (private-read-corrected-windows-tests.log). `go test ./server -run '^Test(JWT|SetupToken)' -count=1 -v -timeout=45s` with disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN passes all nine matched JWT cases, no skips, 2.402s (private-read-jwt-windows-tests.log). Both concurrent startup/duplicate-cleanup cases additionally pass ten repetitions each (3.715s; private-read-jwt-concurrent-windows-tests.log). Session preservation, migration, conflicting copies, hard-linked duplicates, persistent cleanup failure and invalid-key behavior remain covered; the selector matched no separate setup-token tests.

Current Linux privatefs/server binaries cross-compiled with GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 pass in the existing disposable container. The complete privatefs suite has 11 top-level passes, zero failures/skips, including installer and alternate-UID reader opt-ins, exact ordinary bytes, pinned renamed-file identity and swapped FIFO/link cases (private-read-pinned-linux-tests.log). The selected JWT suite has eight top-level passes with no failures/skips (private-read-jwt-pinned-linux-tests.log). These final Linux runs supersede the earlier Lstat-only snapshot version's logs. Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check pass; full application/frontend suites and other operating systems were not tested.

REC-010 remains partial for ancestor replacement/ownership, path-based permission repair, truncation/publication and other independent races. The change does not make shared writable ancestor directories safe or prevent authorized concurrent content writes. Checkpoint remains 33 fixed and 6 open. No new scan/provider worker, dependency installation, deployment or global OS/account change occurred.

## REC-010: Windows installer credential creation (partial follow-up)

Actual hidden Git Bash installer fixtures under an explicit inherited Everyone-full-control parent reproduced broad Windows ACLs on reused .env and new/reused config.json. The new .env fixture also exposed sed replacement syntax treating literal key characters as instructions. Original-source failures are retained in installer-acl-before-tests.log (2.318s); no build, deployment or real credentials were used.

The installer now creates/protects credential destinations before writing. On Windows, private-credential.ps1 uses Windows PowerShell 5.1 and a protected current-user/SYSTEM/Administrators DACL, supplied to the CreateNew FileStream constructor before content. Only the Access section is applied when protecting existing files; the initial all-sections form failed reused-file fixtures and was corrected rather than treated as success. Source: [Microsoft FileStream constructor](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.filestream.-ctor). It rejects reparse-point files, directories and missing parents, preserves reused bytes, and never changes parent permissions. Optional template initialization copies exact bytes only for a newly created destination. Windows release packaging includes the helper; English/Korean manual instructions prepare private files before editing. Installer .env generation writes through the protected file, retaining template lines without sed replacement/backup files or credential values in sed argv. Existing .env contents remain unchanged. Unix uses an exclusive empty-file creation followed by chmod before writes.

Passed: GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./privatefs -count=1 -v -timeout=45s on Windows, explicitly setting SKOPOS_INSTALLER_PERMISSION_TEST=1, SKOPOS_TEST_INSTALLER to this checkout's install.sh and SKOPOS_TEST_BASH to the existing Git Bash executable. Complete suite: 4.880s, no skips; installer-acl-final-windows-tests.log. Four real installer branches use inert Docker/Go stubs, inspect actual DACLs/unchanged parent grants, preserve reused .env bytes, verify literal |/& key data and check no backup remains. Native helper cases preserve UTF-8 template bytes and reused configuration, reject indirect/unsupported destinations and leave outside symlink targets unchanged. A current-source Linux privatefs binary (GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0) passes the complete suite in the existing container, with installer and alternate-UID reader opt-ins enabled; no skips, installer-acl-privatefs-linux-tests.log. All four Unix installer cases pass with unchanged workflows and private modes. Hidden bash -n checks and edited-file gofmt/diff checks pass. Full release archives, full application/frontend suites, separate Windows-user impersonation, hostile path replacement and independent-process/crash races were not tested. No paid scan/provider request, installation, external target, deployment or global OS permission/account change occurred. ## REC-004 / REC-034: Bash invocation option data (partial follow-up)

Six new interceptor regressions reproduced missing restrictive-policy coverage for literal programs invoked through `bash -o pipefail -c`, `-eo pipefail -c`, `-O extglob -c`, `-c -o pipefail`, `+o noexec -c` and `-n +n -c`. The unchanged implementation rejected these option forms or mistook the argument immediately following `-c` for the program. Baseline command `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./intercept -run '^TestRestrictiveRulesSeeStaticShellOperations$/^bash' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s` fails all six new cases (0.788s; shell-option-before-tests.log).

The parser now finds the first non-option command string after scanning supported invocation flags. Known Bash `-o`/`-O` names consume separate option data, including short-option clusters. `--` ends option parsing, and `noexec` tracks explicit enable/disable order through `-n`/`+n` and `-o`/`+o noexec`. Unknown/dynamic names stay unresolved; script paths, missing command strings, positional arguments and syntax-check programs do not become executable subjects. Other shell implementations' named options remain unresolved. Existing raw-input operator overrides and the restriction to deny/ask projections remain unchanged; execution input is never rewritten.

The [GNU Bash invocation documentation](https://www.gnu.org/software/bash/manual/bash.html#Invoking-Bash) specifies the command-string and named-option roles. Independent hidden Git Bash subprocess probes used only `echo SW_OPTION_EXECUTED`: all six bypass option forms and `-co pipefail`, `-eoc pipefail`, `-c --`, `-n +o noexec -c` execute the marker; `-o noexec -c` and `-c -n` do not. Invalid named options and attached `-opipefail` exit 2 without executing the marker. These probes do not execute destructive commands or send HTTP requests.

Passed `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./intercept ./guard -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` with explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN: 28 top-level passes, zero failures/skips (intercept 1.056s, guard 0.984s; shell-option-final-tests.log). Nine new denial cases reach the PostgreSQL-backed seeded production guard for both Bash and shell_send; exact operator-allow and disabled-interception checks remain green. Added negative controls cover noexec, positional program-looking data, invalid/compound/dynamic option names, script-file arguments and missing option/program values. The current interceptor binary cross-compiled with GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 also passes its complete suite in the existing disposable PostgreSQL container, with no skips (shell-option-linux-tests.log). Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check pass. Full application/frontend and Linux guard suites were not rerun.

REC-004/034 remain partial: other wrapper/shell modes, generated programs, external configuration and runtime enforcement are not closed by static argument parsing. Checkpoint remains 33 fixed and 6 open. No new paid scan/provider workers, dependency installation, deployment or global OS change occurred.

## REC-010: duplicate legacy JWT keys (partial follow-up)

When an active signing key already exists, startup now checks any legacy workspace key rather than returning early. Both files are protected before reading. A legacy copy matching the normalized signing material is removed; the active file bytes and sessions remain unchanged. Conflicting/unreadable/invalid legacy keys fail startup without selecting, rotating or deleting either key, with a localized conflict diagnostic that does not echo key material. Directory identity is compared using actual parent-directory file identities, so alternate spellings/symlink/junction aliases cannot cause deletion of the active key. A hard-linked obsolete copy in a different directory is removed only by its legacy name, preserving the active name. Matching leftovers after a previously interrupted publication are handled on restart too.

Initial concurrent tests exposed Windows sharing/deletion-pending races. The JWT loader's native read now permits delete sharing while keeping its reader's file object pinned; bounded Windows retries handle transient sharing/access-denied errors rather than treating them as successful cleanup. Persistent failures still return an error and no key. Unix retains its normal read/unlink semantics without retrying permission failures. Filesystem paths are still assumed operator-owned; this change does not establish a sandbox against hostile concurrent path replacement.

`go test ./server ./privatefs ./locale -run 'TestJWTKey|TestReadSetupToken|TestPrivateFile|TestProductionCatalog' -count=1 -v -timeout 30s` passes on Windows against the explicit disposable config/database (2.409s / 0.308s / 0.218s), with no skips. Real JWT signatures survive duplicate cleanup/restart; conflict cases preserve both private files without diagnostic disclosure; directory aliases/hard-linked copies, concurrent initial migration and existing-key cleanup all pass. A permanently held normal Windows read handle proves failed deletion remains fail-closed (0.54s). The initial read-only-attribute fixture was incorrect because Go's Windows os.Remove clears that attribute; it was replaced with a genuine persistent sharing violation while retaining the failure assertion. Logs: jwt-duplicate-private-windows-tests.log, jwt-duplicate-private-final-windows-tests.log.

The observed concurrency race justified repeating only `go test ./server -run '^TestJWTKeyConcurrentDuplicateCleanup$' -count=10 -timeout 30s`; all ten runs pass (2.355s), with twelve simultaneous calls in each migration/existing-copy case. Log: jwt-duplicate-concurrency-windows-tests.log. ## REC-010: legacy task archives and staging (partial follow-up)

Manager startup now repairs the owned archives/tasks tree before recovery, covering legacy packages, interrupted staging/restoration and pending-delete packages. A privatefs archive variant preserves regular files' owner execute/read-only bits on Unix while removing group/world access; Windows applies protected private DACLs without changing the read-only attribute. Owned directories become private. Permission repair skips links/nonregular entries without following their targets; the root must be a genuine directory. The archive writer retains its existing symlink handling. Shared workspace/data-directory ancestor permissions and payload bytes remain unchanged.

Staging protects its root before writing/reading journals, and repairs source payload permissions before rename (or repairs a destination already present during replay). Rollback retains the repaired private permissions and owner execution, rather than making sensitive restored files public again. Restore staging and extraction destinations receive private directory ACLs before content creation. Legacy/external packages are protected before reading, preserving owner read-only mode. Recovery/staging journals must be regular private files before reads; linked journals are rejected rather than following arbitrary targets. Crash recovery/rollback semantics and file formats are unchanged; hostile concurrent path swaps and authority retained by owners/privileged OS accounts are not claimed solved.
