## REC-010: finding evidence storage and Windows publication (partial follow-up)

Evidence staging files and report/archive body copies now use native private creation before writing. Application-owned evidence root/staging/blob directories use private modes/DACLs; copy destinations protect only their own root/blob directories. Existing body verification first protects the regular file, so legacy deduplication and reads also remove unrelated grants. NewManager repairs the entire owned evidence tree once at startup before database access, covering legacy bodies not requested this session. Its shared data-directory permissions and evidence bytes stay unchanged. Symlink/nonregular entries in that owned tree fail startup rather than being traversed. Standalone Store callers get per-file read/write protection without a repeated whole-tree scan.

Current-code diagnosis reproduced the earlier Windows finding failures: the body was flushed/closed and renamed, then directory Sync returned Access is denied. Publication is now platform-specific. Unix retains rename followed by directory fsync. Windows uses native MoveFileExW with REPLACE_EXISTING and WRITE_THROUGH after the body itself has been synced/closed; COPY_ALLOWED is intentionally absent, so a cross-volume copy cannot reset the private descriptor. This avoids the unsupported POSIX directory-sync path rather than ignoring an arbitrary write error. Microsoft documents the move/write-through flags and the ACL change for cross-volume moves: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-movefileexw. No equivalent Unix directory-fsync guarantee, crash/power-loss simulation or hostile path-replacement isolation is claimed for Windows.

Actual ACL checks under a public parent verify new evidence publication, legacy-body read repair, exported body copies and unchanged parent permissions/raw bytes. Long paths, deduplication, atomic failure cases, concurrent bind/delete, GC grace/restore, and startup legacy-tree repair pass. All six formerly failing Windows evidence cases now pass: finding API/export, archive V3 retry, UTF8/inherited writes, localized raw bytes, planner auto-hint and reporter binding. ## REC-010: new archive/export file privacy (partial follow-up)

Extraction creates private regular files before copying bytes and restores only the archive header's owner permission bits. Windows uses the protected DACL plus normal owner-mode/read-only handling. This does not establish hostile concurrent path-replacement isolation or harden already moved source files/directories in archive staging. ## REC-010: standalone JWT key permissions (partial follow-up)

New JWT keys are created in a sibling temporary file with owner-only Unix mode or a protected native Windows DACL supplied to CreateFile before any secret bytes are written. After write/sync/close, a hard link publishes the complete key without overwriting an existing destination; concurrent startup losers read the winning key. Temporary files are removed on ordinary completion/failure. This requires a filesystem supporting hard links: unsupported filesystems fail startup rather than silently weakening permissions or atomic publication. A process crash can leave a private temporary file; parent-directory durability and hostile filesystem replacement races are not claimed solved.

Existing regular keys have their permissions tightened before reading, without rewriting their bytes or hardening the executable directory. Legacy workspace migration preserves signing material and deletes the source after successful publication. Invalid/unreadable keys now fail closed rather than silently generate replacements; an operator must repair or intentionally reset invalid material. Symlinks/nonregular files are rejected. Existing unrelated duplicate legacy files when a destination already exists, config files outside the Unix installer, and external archive destinations remain open REC-010 work. Windows SYSTEM/Administrators and filesystem owners retain their usual recovery authority.

Real JWT signatures remain valid after restart and migration; twelve concurrent startup calls return one stored key; invalid key evidence remains intact. Logs: jwt-private-permissions-tests.log. ## REC-010: owned traffic permissions and Unix installer credentials (partial)

Current-code review confirmed the blob helper now creates private POSIX files, but traffic's outer directories and legacy/restored content still inherited earlier permissions. Installer .env copies/reuse and config.json redirection also lacked explicit private permissions. Skopos now protects only its owned traffic root before opening SQLite or loading/creating its CA. A small privatefs helper repairs existing regular files/directories without rewriting their bytes: Unix files become 0600 and directories 0700; Windows uses native x/sys/windows DACL APIs to remove inherited/explicit unrelated grants and permit the process user, SYSTEM and Administrators. Protected directory ACLs carry object/container inheritance for new children. Existing directories are walked once; subsequent standard subdirectory creation uses owner-only Unix modes or that Windows inheritance. Symlinks/unsupported entries reject initialization instead of walking into unrelated targets. Permission failures propagate; no workspace/repository/executable ancestor is recursively hardened. x/sys was already pinned and is now explicitly marked as a direct dependency; no package was downloaded.

On Unix, install.sh sets umask 077 before credential creation and explicitly repairs .env/config.json to 0600, including reused files. Existing config.json is tightened before truncation/writing, and reused .env contents remain unchanged. Symlinked credential destinations are rejected. This shell-mode repair does not establish Windows installer ACL protection. A private enclosing traffic directory also protects newly created SQLite/CA files whose library-level leaf modes may be broader; this change does not claim every future library file is individually 0600. Startup repairs existing leaf files. Local privileged administrators/root and original filesystem owners retain their OS authority; this is not a sandbox against privileged or already-open file handles.

## REC-027: recording-proxy transport peer authorization

The retained source describes lower-trust clients reaching Docker's separate :8788 listener and forwarding/tunneling into the backend network without application authorization. Current-code inspection confirms the proxy runs independently of management JWT middleware and remains listening when capture routing is off. A capture toggle is not presented as a listener firewall.

Skopos now always supplies the pinned native proxy with a transport-level ClientAllowed callback. Every accepted TCP connection is checked before HTTP parsing, CONNECT dialing, TLS certificate work or addon callbacks. Unauthorized peers are closed and accepting continues for trusted clients. The decision uses the actual net.Addr, never X-Forwarded-For, Forwarded, Host, task metadata or a proxy credential header. IPv4, IPv6, mapped IPv4 and IPv6 zone metadata are handled by network identity. Loopback (127/8 and ::1) remains trusted for same-host workers/browser clients; additional remote clients require explicit startup SKOPOS_PROXY_CLIENT_CIDRS grants. Invalid CIDRs refuse proxy initialization before its store is created. The vendor callback's nil default preserves other upstream callers; traffic.Open always installs it. Original upstream hashes/licenses remain in UPSTREAM.json; the patch/upgrade notes now include proxy/entry.go.

This is operator-approved network-client authorization. Local processes share loopback trust, and authorized subnets/NAT/forwarders represent every client behind them. It is not individual JWT authentication, a network sandbox or REC-022's task-specific destination authorization. Neither TLS passthrough nor the capture toggle can bypass the client gate. Established authorized connections retain their admission; grants change on restart. Compose still publishes host loopback, passes the new environment field, and requires an explicit trusted bridge/client source grant for manual host-to-container proxy use. Do not treat the host loopback publication as proof that the container sees a loopback peer. Both READMEs and .env.example document that migration and the trust implications; no universal bridge CIDR is automatically trusted.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local SKOPOS_PROXY_TEST_CLIENT_IP=<verified local non-loopback IPv4> go test ./traffic ./locale -count=1 -v -timeout 60s` (9.233s / 0.536s), no skips. Against a real wildcard listener, default-unapproved non-loopback clients cannot complete HTTP, CONNECT, forced passthrough or WebSocket requests; target connection counters remain unchanged. Spoofed forwarding headers do not help. An explicit /32 grant allows complete HTTP responses, verified HTTPS through CONNECT/MITM with a synthetic trusted target CA, passthrough CONNECT 200 and a genuine WebSocket 101. Loopback clients remain operational after rejection. CIDR identity and invalid-configuration-before-store tests pass, along with the full traffic capture/search/storage/archive/TLS regressions. Log: proxy-client-tests.log. The test's opt-in interface is validated to belong to the host; no external target is used.

Cross-compiled the same traffic test binary with GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 and ran `-test.run=^TestProxyClient -test.v=true -test.timeout=30s` in the existing disposable PostgreSQL container, using its actual 172.17.0.2 bridge interface. All client-policy cases pass, with no skips; transport admission completed in 0.21s. This verifies Linux/container socket behavior on a real bridge interface, not a second-container attack or public deployment. The first shell invocation split unquoted dotted test flags; quoted single-token flags corrected it. Log: proxy-client-linux-tests.log.

`SKOPOS_PROXY_CLIENT_E2E=1 SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_STORAGE_E2E=1 go test ./server -run 'TestRecordingClientPolicySurvivesCaptureToggle|TestTrafficBlobStorageStatusAPI' -count=1 -v -timeout 30s` passes against the explicit disposable skopos_transport_security database/configuration with provider environment settings cleared. The real manager rejects non-loopback peers with capture off/on/off, preserves enabled worker routing and trusted loopback access, and retains authenticated storage API behavior. Logs: proxy-client-manager-tests.log. These bounded test processes own their listeners; manager shutdown's broader listener lifecycle is not claimed verified.

A source overlay disabling only accepted-peer enforcement fails the same denied HTTP regression with an actual request reaching the proxy (1.224s). This is a fault injection, not an exact-HEAD baseline. Log: proxy-client-negative/result.log. gofmt, diff checks and Compose JSON inspection pass. No additional paid scan/model request, image rebuild, public deployment, user network setting change or external target interaction occurred. Full server/frontend suites were not rerun; known Windows baseline failures remain unresolved.

## REC-022: direct resolved HTTP peer policy (partial follow-up)

Actual native HTTP execution reproduced a resolved-address bypass: an allowed localhost URL reached a loopback fixture even though the task blocked both 127.0.0.0/8 and ::1/128. URL-only checks evaluated the domain and missed the actual resolved IP. `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^TestHTTPResolvedDestinationBlock$' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s`, with explicit disposable SKOPOS_CONFIG/SKOPOS_PG_DSN and SKOPOS_DESTINATION_E2E=1, fails with one receiver request and a successful HTTP result (1.590s; http-peer-before-tests.log).

AssetStore.CheckHTTPPeer now evaluates global/task restrictive IP rules independently of URL/domain grants. Direct native requests resolve a dial attempt once, check each candidate and pass only an approved numeric address to the TCP dialer; there is no second hostname resolution between IP policy and that dial. Domain/URL approval need not duplicate every DNS answer as an IP allow rule, but cannot override IP blocks. Candidate families use bounded dual-stack fallback (two serial family dialers, 300ms delayed fallback or immediate fallback on failure), with cancellation/losing-connection cleanup. TLS retains the original hostname and trust settings. Each direct request/hop uses a fresh transport/connection so a prior peer decision cannot bypass new restrictive rules. Direct custom TLS-dial hooks cannot bypass this TCP path.

URL policy is re-read after DNS resolution immediately before checking/dialing each candidate; the URL check is also repeated before proxy forwarding. A synthetic callback revoking a URL grant during lookup proves no receiver request and a typed policy error. This is a deterministic callback-boundary test, not a real database mutation during a DNS response. The real PostgreSQL fixture proves blocked resolved loopback sends zero HTTP requests, preserves Korean policy rendering without its synthetic query token, and succeeds using the same URL grant after deleting the two IP blocks. Structured peer policy errors are extracted from net/http wrappers, including mixed candidate failures, so policy diagnostics omit userinfo/query URLs.

Proxy routing retains its existing behavior: proxy infrastructure is not interpreted as the target IP, and the proxy owns target resolution. A real local proxy fixture verifies forwarding without local target resolution or applying target-IP rules to its infrastructure. This does not enforce the proxy's upstream DNS/IP choice. Resolver/dial fixtures prove one lookup, numeric-address pinning and filtering without dialing a blocked candidate; dual-stack fallback cancels a stalled first-family dial. A real local TLS fixture proves original SNI/hostname validation and rejects a wrong hostname; it uses an explicitly trusted fixture CA, not disabled verification.

Final Windows command `go test ./server -run '^Test(HTTPPeer.*|HTTPResolvedDestinationBlock|HTTPDestination.*|WorkerHTTPDestinationPolicyWithoutIntentAssets|RunHTTPTool|HTTPToolTruncatesLargeBody|Custom.*Redirect.*)$' -count=1 -v -timeout=90s` with GOTOOLCHAIN=local and the explicit disposable config/transport database opt-in passes 12 top-level tests, zero failures/skips, 4.258s (http-peer-revocation-windows-tests.log). The current server test binary cross-compiled with GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 passes the same selection in the existing disposable PostgreSQL container: 12 top-level passes, no failures/skips (http-peer-revocation-linux-tests.log). This includes the existing 36 bilingual/streaming actual-worker SDK cases, initial/redirect grants and revocation, unavailable-policy failure, HTTP bodies/truncation and resolved redirect-operation review. Earlier focused/compatibility runs are retained separately; the final runs include the added post-DNS callback check.

English/Korean documentation and the status row reflect direct resolved-IP enforcement. Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check pass. Full application/frontend, macOS, packet capture, general executor network isolation and proxy-mediated target enforcement were not tested. REC-022 remains partial for proxied DNS/IP routing, shell/Python/MCP, legacy intent/runtime boundaries and broader isolation. Checkpoint remains 33 fixed and 6 open. No new paid scan/provider worker, dependencies, deployment, host DNS edits or global OS changes occurred.

## REC-022: native HTTP execution destination policy (partial)

Current-code inspection confirms add_intent checks asset policy only when asset_ids is nonempty. Worker execution reads those IDs for context but does not independently constrain a summary's actual operations. Requiring model-authored IDs alone would not establish a network boundary and would disrupt unbound reconnaissance. This family remains open.

The native custom HTTP executor now evaluates the actual rendered request URL against global blocks, task blocks and the task allowlist, using the code-owned RunInfo.TaskID. Every redirect is independently checked before replay. Intent asset_ids, arbitrary target parameters, coverage proposals, read grants and association metadata are not authorization witnesses. The current configured rule behavior is preserved: block before allow, and an empty or entirely disabled allowlist imposes no allow restriction. Policy is loaded on each check; missing task policy storage or a failed database read denies the request. Destination checks run before ordinary operation review and again after it returns, so a grant revoked during approval cannot be overridden by the operation's allow verdict. Non-task manager-backed HTTP tools apply global blocks. These checks do not depend on whether tool interception or its judge is enabled. Existing operation review and redirect semantics remain intact.

Redirect-review failures now display the localizable review error directly rather than losing it inside net/http's url.Error wrapper, which includes the redirect URL. The tested policy denial avoids echoing a synthetic query token. Rule patterns and notes still render as operator-configured policy messages; this is not a universal HTTP diagnostic redaction claim.

Passed: `SKOPOS_DESTINATION_E2E=1 GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server ./locale -run 'TestWorkerHTTPDestinationPolicyWithoutIntentAssets|TestHTTPDestination.*FailsClosed|TestCustomHTTPRedirectsReviewEffectiveMethodBodyAndDestination|TestProductionCatalogCompleteAndFormatCompatible' -count=1 -v -timeout 60s` (3.554s / 0.550s), no skips, with explicit isolated config and the disposable skopos_transport_security PostgreSQL database. Thirty-six actual Worker -> SDK -> custom HTTP executions use a synthetic provider across EN/KO and streaming/non-streaming: approved destinations, denied initial targets/redirects, no allowlist, task/global blocks, disabled allow rules, grants revoked by the start server before redirect, and grants revoked inside an approving operation reviewer. Intent asset_ids is omitted and arbitrary tool metadata supplies forged target/asset values; denied receivers observe zero requests. Allowed cases receive the original response. Missing task storage and a closed policy database fail before network delivery. Existing effective method/body/redirect review and catalog formatting regressions pass. The initial fixture mistakenly used double-brace placeholders instead of this executor's single-brace syntax; correcting the fixture exposed a real localization defect in redirect wrapping, repaired above.

Fault-injection overlays remove only the native destination check or only its post-review recheck. The identical denied-request and revocation regressions fail with actual requests reaching the synthetic targets. These are fault injections, not exact-HEAD baselines. Logs: execution-destination-tests.log and execution-destination-negative/ under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/. gofmt and diff checks passed. Full server/frontend suites were not rerun; the known Windows baseline failures remain unresolved. No paid scan/model request, external target or deployment was used.

Remaining requirements include independent target enforcement for arbitrary shell/Python programs, interactive processes, SDK network tools, MCP and extensions; DNS/IP rebinding and proxy routes; admission of legacy/unbound intents; and the resulting operator workflow. This native URL gate is not an OS/network sandbox and does not prove those controls. REC-022 stays partial; the checkpoint remains 32 fixed and seven open.

## REC-024: incoming WAL write admission (partial follow-up)

An unchanged-source capture regression reproduced a transaction-size gap: with an empty WAL and a 262144-byte admission threshold, an exactly-inline 256 KiB binary response grew the WAL to 321392 bytes. `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic -run '^TestWALAdmissionAccountsForIncomingCapture$' -count=1 -v -timeout=30s` fails (1.280s; wal-incoming-before-tests.log). The prior between-transaction check considered only existing WAL bytes, so the entire incoming operation bypassed the threshold while the WAL was empty.

Capture and missing-row import admission now include their existing conservative index-write estimate in the WAL headroom decision. Incoming writes whose estimate alone exceeds the allowance fail before transaction/blob publication with a distinct localized ErrWALWriteEstimate. Otherwise the existing non-waiting checkpoint/retry path runs when current WAL plus incoming estimate does not fit. SQLite transaction headroom retains 128 KiB, reduced to half the configured threshold for small allowances so the supported 64 KiB minimum still captures small evidence. An intermediate 32 KiB allowance was insufficient for observed SQLite/FTS growth in the pinned-reader fixture; the failure is retained in wal-incoming-corrected-tests.log and the final implementation uses the larger reserve. This is conservative admission, not an independent hard limit on actual SQLite writes.

The pinned-reader fixture now fills using small writes until admission rejects an incoming estimate rather than requiring the old WAL to reach/exceed the threshold first. It still checks the pinned snapshot's exact row count and now checks that the fixture itself stays below its allowance. Snapshot/blob-import and real-proxy fixtures use 524288-byte allowances so their operations fit an empty WAL; their purpose is reader-induced blocking and subsequent resume, rather than permanent oversized-operation rejection. The proxy assertion records the fixture's initial dropped count and requires exactly one additional loss for the real request, retaining byte-for-byte target response checks. A separate unchanged 262144-byte fixture proves oversized incoming capture rejection, and a new oversized-import fixture proves no partial rows/WAL/blobs plus exact idempotent retry after increasing its test capacity. Minimum-threshold small capture and Korean error rendering also pass. Intermediate fixture/headroom failures in wal-incoming-focused-tests.log and wal-incoming-corrected-tests.log remain visible.

Passed final focused command: `go test ./traffic -run '^TestWALAdmission' -count=1 -v -timeout=45s`, 1.846s (wal-incoming-headroom-tests.log). Full Windows `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic ./locale -p 1 -count=1 -v -timeout=120s` passes 81 top-level tests, zero failures, one transport opt-in skip (traffic 10.175s, locale 0.422s; wal-incoming-final-windows-tests.log). The current traffic binary cross-compiled with GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0 passes its complete suite in the existing disposable container: 69 top-level passes, zero failures, the same TestProxyClientTransportAdmission opt-in skip (wal-incoming-final-linux-tests.log). Proxy response preservation, blob/index admission, capture/search, archive streaming/import/retry, snapshots/concurrent deletion, reclamation and localized errors remain covered by these suites. That separate transport opt-in and full application/frontend/macOS suites were not run for this change.

English/Korean documentation now explains incoming estimates, empty-WAL rejection/retry and the remaining hard-limit gap. Edited-file gofmt and git diff --check pass. REC-024 remains partial for actual per-transaction overshoot beyond estimates, multi-process writers, temporary/legacy/staging storage and aggregate disk limits. Checkpoint remains 33 fixed and 6 open. No new scan/provider worker, dependency, deployment, operator capacity change or global OS change occurred; capacity changes above affect disposable test fixtures only.

## REC-024: persistent traffic blob budget (partial)

The large-body store now has a default 1 GiB byte budget, configurable at process startup through positive integer SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_BLOB_BYTES. Compose passes the variable and .env.example documents its default. Open inventories existing regular blob files once, so restart cannot forget their usage; existing evidence above a reduced limit stays readable, and deduplicated bodies add no bytes. Capture and archive import use the same write-lock-protected admission and atomic file-publication helper. New files are written to a temporary file, synced/closed and renamed before becoming referenced evidence. Old captures are never evicted automatically. Failed response storage, index transactions and imports remove only freshly created unreferenced blobs; an uncertain reference lookup retains the file rather than risking evidence deletion. Garbage collection releases capacity after deletion succeeds. New blob files use private POSIX creation modes; Windows ACL and the other REC-010 paths remain separate work.

An over-budget capture is skipped as a whole, with a localized log and dropped-capture count; there is no fabricated successful truncated body. The target response continues unchanged through the recording proxy. Archive import returns an explicit budget error and rolls back fresh writes, permitting a retry after the operator increases capacity or removes traffic. GET /api/traffic exposes blob_storage with limit_bytes, used_bytes and dropped_captures (the latter counts loss since process start). This is blob-file payload usage, not total disk usage; SQLite, FTS, WAL, legacy trees, filesystem allocation overhead, archive staging/export and multi-process/external filesystem writes are not covered by this component.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic ./locale -count=1 -v -timeout 60s` (9.317s / 0.632s), with no skips. New tests cover exact capacity, deduplication, restart accounting, eight concurrent rejected captures, deletion/recovery, request/response rollback, injected SQLite index failure cleanup, failed archive import and idempotent successful retry, absence of leaked atomic-write temporary files, invalid configuration and Korean quota errors. A real local proxy/HTTP target test verifies the client receives every original response byte despite recording rejection. The full traffic suite retains existing capture/search/blob retrieval, shared/legacy deletion, archives, streaming export, rollback/recovery and upstream TLS checks. Log: traffic-blob-budget-tests.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

Passed: `SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_STORAGE_E2E=1 go test ./server -run '^TestTrafficBlobStorageStatusAPI$' -count=1 -v -timeout 30s` (2.190s), using isolated SKOPOS_CONFIG and disposable skopos_transport_security PostgreSQL, with provider environment variables explicitly cleared. The production manager/API route and actual imported blob file return the correct authenticated storage limit/usage. Log: traffic-blob-budget-api-tests.log. The whole server/frontend suites were not rerun; the seven existing Windows server baseline failures remain unresolved. No frontend code was changed for this follow-up.

A source overlay disabling only quota admission fails the identical restart/concurrent-capture regression (1.314s), demonstrating extra evidence rows are accepted. This is a fault injection, not an exact-HEAD baseline. Log: blob-budget-negative/result.log. `POSTGRES_PASSWORD=compose-validation-only docker compose config --format json` plus JSON inspection confirms the default environment value; no application image was rebuilt or deployed. gofmt and `git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check` passed.


### SQLite index allocation and admission follow-up

Traffic indexes now default to a 1 GiB allocation limit, configured at process startup with SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_INDEX_BYTES (minimum 262144 bytes). A private database/sql connector wraps the existing registered SQLite driver and sets max_page_count from the actual page size on every newly opened pooled connection. It preserves the original driver connection and optional interfaces, global function registrations, WAL mode and archive-read concurrency; it does not install a global hook or constrain other SQLite databases. The bare-filename DSN path also receives its own connection-local busy timeout and allocation ceiling. Existing files above a lowered limit are preserved: SQLite cannot reduce max_page_count below the current page count, so they remain grandfathered rather than silently truncated. Existing oversized pooled connections can retain their grandfathered ceiling until recycled; application write admission still uses the configured limit.

Before capture writes bodies or begins its index transaction, conservative admission estimates inline/indexed bytes and reserves maintenance space. Archive admission checks only missing exchange IDs, includes full imported index text, and rejects before copying fresh blobs; a complete idempotent retry adds no estimated index cost. This admission is a conservative capacity policy, not an exact prediction of SQLite/FTS allocation. SQLite's page-count ceiling separately produces SQLITE_FULL for allocations above the connection's limit. Storage refusal logs a localized reason and increments the existing dropped-capture count, while reads and explicit cleanup remain available. Compose and .env.example pass/document both index and blob limits.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic ./locale -count=1 -v -timeout 60s` (10.144s / 0.521s), with no skips. Three simultaneously checked-out physical connections each expose the configured page-count limit and reject an oversized native SQLite write specifically with SQLITE_FULL (code 13). Additional tests cover fill/admission refusal, accepted-evidence preservation across restart, purge/compaction and resumed recording, denied index imports followed by a higher-limit restart/retry, lowered limits on existing oversized data, and invalid settings. All previous blob quota, live proxy, capture/search, archives/export concurrency, deletion/recovery and TLS cases also pass. Log: traffic-index-budget-tests.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

Passed: `SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_STORAGE_E2E=1 go test ./server -run '^TestTrafficBlobStorageStatusAPI$' -count=1 -v -timeout 30s` (1.866s), against isolated configuration and disposable skopos_transport_security, with all provider environment settings cleared. The test now cancels its API context before manager teardown; the archive worker reports context cancellation during shutdown. The earlier attempt logged a closed-database teardown race; neither is a capture/quota failure, and this focused run does not establish full server shutdown correctness. Log: traffic-index-budget-api-tests.log.

A fault-injection overlay replacing only the connector's bounded max_page_count with SQLite's maximum fails the identical physical-connection ceiling assertion (1.252s). This is not an exact-HEAD baseline. Log: index-budget-negative/result.log. Compose configuration plus JSON inspection confirms the index limit environment value; no image was rebuilt/deployed. gofmt and `git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check` passed. Full server/frontend suites were not rerun; the seven previously verified Windows server baseline failures remain open.

[SQLite's max_page_count documentation](https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_max_page_count) supports the main-database allocation boundary. Its [journal_size_limit documentation](https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html#pragma_journal_size_limit) describes post-transaction/checkpoint file reuse/truncation, not a hard active-WAL quota. No journal pragma is presented as complete storage enforcement. Active WAL, SQLite temporary files, existing legacy trees, archive staging/export and aggregate/multi-process disk budgets still require separate work under REC-024.

REC-024 remains partial until the remaining persistent storage consumers are bounded and their cleanup/import workflows verified. Thirty-two families remain fixed and seven open. All traffic and credentials used here were synthetic local fixtures; no paid scan/model provider request was made.

### Active WAL admission follow-up

Traffic now checks the physical index.sqlite-wal size under the existing write mutex before admitting a capture or a new-row archive import. SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_WAL_BYTES defaults to 128 MiB (minimum 65536 bytes). At the threshold it attempts a passive checkpoint, then truncation only when all frames have been checkpointed, using a checked-out connection with a temporary zero busy timeout restored to 5000 ms afterward. It does not wait for or terminate an archive snapshot. If reclamation cannot reduce the WAL below the threshold, capture logs a localized failure and counts the dropped capture; imports return a localized error before publishing fresh blobs or rows. Later writes retry automatically after the snapshot releases. The authenticated traffic API reports actual WAL bytes and the configured admission threshold. Compose, .env.example and both READMEs document this behavior.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic ./locale -count=1 -v -timeout 60s` (12.273s / 0.558s), no skips. A real pinned archive SQL transaction preserves its original snapshot while later captures reach the threshold; twenty further capture attempts leave row count, blob usage and physical WAL size unchanged. Pooled busy_timeout is restored, and capture resumes after release. Archive import rejects before changing rows/blobs, then imports and retries idempotently after release. Invalid configuration and Korean error text are covered. An actual local HTTP proxy still forwards the full target response while WAL admission rejects its capture. Log: traffic-wal-budget-tests.log.

`SKOPOS_TRAFFIC_STORAGE_E2E=1 go test ./server -run '^TestTrafficBlobStorageStatusAPI$' -count=1 -v -timeout 30s` passed (2.028s), using the explicitly isolated skopos_transport_security PostgreSQL database/configuration with providers cleared and no model work. Both actual blob usage and WAL status match the authenticated API response. Log: traffic-wal-budget-api-tests.log. The first import fixture used a colliding ID from independent stores' existing per-second counters and exercised an idempotent retry; assigning the source fixture a disjoint counter range corrected the test without changing product ID behavior.

A fault-injection overlay removing only the WAL admission call fails the same pinned-reader regression (1.232s): physical WAL grows from 317272 to 1825192 bytes instead of stopping. This is not an exact-HEAD baseline. Log: wal-budget-negative/result.log. gofmt and diff checks passed; Compose JSON inspection confirms the default WAL environment value. No Docker image build/deployment, full server/frontend rerun or paid scan/model request occurred.

This is admission between transactions, not a hard active-transaction WAL quota: an already-admitted capture/import can exceed the threshold. Cleanup remains available and is not subject to capture admission. SQLite temporary files, legacy data, archive staging/export and aggregate/multi-process disk usage remain unbounded by these settings. REC-024 remains partial; the current checkpoint is still 32 families fixed and seven open.

## REC-003: shell quote context and Windows argument preservation

The earlier single-pass repair prevented replacement text from becoming another placeholder, but wrapping every value in POSIX single quotes was unsafe inside existing template quotes and did not match Windows execution. Inspection of norma v0.4.3 tool/task.go confirms its Bash-labelled tool runs PowerShell on Windows and bash elsewhere. Custom command rendering now follows that actual shell.

Bash uses the already-pinned mvdan syntax parser to identify literal spans and chooses escaping for bare, single-quoted, double-quoted and ANSI-quoted data. Windows passes an inert randomized-marker template on stdin to PowerShell's own Parser.ParseInput and classifies literal StringConstant/ExpandableString AST nodes; real parameter values never enter that parser's command line or code. Single/double/bare literals use PowerShell-specific quoting, including smart quotes. No template code is evaluated during classification. The parser process is hidden on Windows, inherits request cancellation, and has a 15-second limit. Final resolved command bytes still enter the existing operation-review/SDK executor boundary. HTTP interpolation remains unchanged and single-pass. No dependency was added for this repair.

Placeholder substitution into parameter expansion, arithmetic, legacy Bash backticks, here-doc/here-string bodies or unmatched escapes is rejected before execution rather than pretending argument escaping can safely generate syntax. Plain unconfigured placeholders remain untouched. Supported nested command substitution data, adjacent argument prefixes/suffixes, JSON array serialization, Unicode and literal placeholder-like parameter values are covered. Operators should place placeholders in literal arguments; Python script tools continue to receive structured JSON stdin rather than shell interpolation. New errors have English/Korean messages.

Passed real Bash tests: 25 scalar round trips across five quote/substitution forms plus a JSON-array/prefix/suffix case. Passed actual PowerShell tests: 12 managed-command scalar round trips, including Unicode/smart quotes and expansion-looking values, plus rejected non-data contexts and cancellation. Actual SDK execution also preserves quoted fixture strings in Windows managed commands. Source overlays restoring only the old context-independent renderer fail identical Bash and PowerShell round-trip assertions as expected (2.214s); this is a fault injection, not an exact-HEAD baseline. Logs: quoted-command-template-tests.log, quoted-command-final-tests.log and quoted-template-negative/result.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

### Windows native literal argument follow-up

The previous embedded-quote regression is now repaired. Real Windows PowerShell 5.1 probes showed that native handoff needs a complete quoted argument, balanced pairs of embedded quotes, and doubled backslashes before quotes and at the argument end. Encoding each placeholder independently breaks adjacent prefixes/suffixes and multiple placeholders. The renderer instead replaces each supported native literal AST argument as a whole, then applies single-pass substitution to the original marker program. Application resolution uses the same working directory and extra environment as the SDK executor; local function declarations are excluded, and command-path tokens are not native data arguments. Unsupported here-strings remain rejected. Managed PowerShell commands retain their own quoting. Execution and final review still go through the existing SDK; no separate executor or dependency was added.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|Custom(ConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule|.*Operation|.*Redirect.*|.*Resolved.*)|RenderTemplate)' -count=1 -v -timeout 90s` (30.069s), with explicit SKOPOS_TEST_PYTHON, isolated SKOPOS_CONFIG and disposable SKOPOS_PG_DSN pointing to semantic_sdk_operations_20261010. The run includes 24 real SDK -> PowerShell -> Python native argv cases (bare/single/double quote forms, empty strings, spaces, trailing backslashes, backslash/quote combinations, consecutive quotes, multiline strings, Unicode, literal placeholder-looking values, prefixes/suffixes and two placeholders). Task environment regressions preserve injected PATH/environment, absolute and relative application resolution, working directory, pipeline output and file writes. Real Bash/managed PowerShell round trips, rejection/cancellation and all 48 command/script/HTTP approval cases in both languages also pass. No skips were recorded. Log: quoted-native-final-tests.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

A fault-injection overlay disabling only native classification fails the identical embedded-quote/argument-boundary regression (1.930s); this is not an exact-HEAD baseline. Log: quoted-native-negative/result.log. Prior failed candidate and original-loss logs remain historical evidence. Earlier custom-operation runs that omitted SKOPOS_TEST_PYTHON skipped resolved-operation coverage; the final run above explicitly enables and executes it.

### Windows runtime-expanded native argument follow-up

The previously failing `${env:SWT}` plus placeholder native-argument case is now repaired. For an identified native argument, parameter data is escaped for its actual PowerShell double-quoted context, and the existing template-owned runtime expression evaluates before a fixed encoding script block receives the completed string. The encoder balances quotes and doubles backslashes before quotes and at the argument end. Native literals use the same fixed encoder. The script block resolves command kind before evaluating that argument's expression and returns ordinary string data if a template redirects the application name to a managed alias. Parameter text is never evaluated as PowerShell code, template expressions keep caller scope, and the whole final command remains subject to the existing operation review and SDK execution. The unused Go-only native encoder was removed.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./server -run '^Test(CommandTemplate.*|PowerShellCommandTemplateRoundTrip|Custom(ConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule|.*Operation|.*Redirect.*|.*Resolved.*)|RenderTemplate)' -count=1 -v -timeout 90s` (45.201s), with the same isolated config, disposable semantic_sdk_operations_20261010 database and explicit Python fixture. Added 24 real native argv cases cover environment expansion, caller variables and template-owned subexpressions with quotes, backslashes, empty/multiline/Unicode parameter strings. The subexpression counter confirms exactly one evaluation. Two native-to-managed alias cases preserve ordinary managed values, with and without environment expansion. All earlier 24 literal-native cases, task PATH/directory/pipeline/file-output checks, managed PowerShell and Bash round trips, rejection/cancellation, and 48 bilingual SDK approval cases pass. No skips occurred. Log: quoted-native-runtime-final-tests.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

A source overlay disabling only runtime-expanded native classification fails the identical environment-expansion/native argv assertion (2.112s). Log: quoted-native-runtime-negative/result.log. This is a fault injection, not an exact-HEAD baseline. The old quoted-native-mixed-expansion failure log remains historical evidence of the repaired path.

REC-003 completion remains unproven for dynamic executable expressions, templates changing a previously managed/unknown command into a native application, native arguments that contain additional parameter placeholders within nested runtime expressions, and applications with non-CRT argument parsers. Those paths currently retain ordinary PowerShell quoting. These are concrete compatibility boundaries to verify and repair, not a sandbox claim. The full server/frontend suites were not rerun; the seven previously reproduced Windows server baseline failures remain unresolved. `git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check` passed.

REC-003 remains partial. Thirty-one families remain fixed and eight open. No additional paid scan/model worker, external target, real credential or provider request was used.

## REC-004 / REC-034: static operation subjects for restrictive rules

Confirmed: seeded deny rules matched only raw JSON text. Equivalent forms such as curl --request=DELETE and quoted executable fragments bypassed those enabled rules. Interceptor now adds a static parsed operation view for Bash command, shell_open command, shell_send text and resolved WebFetch method. Existing ordered rules still run against the original input first. Only deny/ask tool_input rules can additionally match the parsed view; allow rules do not gain new matches. Original bytes, digests, pending approval and execution inputs are preserved. Explicit higher-priority allows and deliberately disabled tool interception remain honored.

The added pinned mvdan.cc/sh/v3 v3.14.1 dependency supplies Bash/POSIX syntax parsing rather than attempting quote removal with another regex. Static word decoding rejects parameter, arithmetic and command/process substitutions, uses an empty environment and leaves filesystem globbing disabled. Syntax walking separately sees actual nested executable substitutions; it never executes them. Only known safety-relevant executable names produce subjects. Direct curl/wget method projections handle quoted/concatenated spellings, attached short options, --request= forms, common boolean short-option clusters and long argument distances. Header/body values are kept distinct from method options; literal echo/here-document content and file writes are not reparsed as executable commands. Parser and expansion behavior were checked against [official syntax documentation](https://pkg.go.dev/mvdan.cc/sh/v3/syntax) and [pinned expansion documentation](https://pkg.go.dev/mvdan.cc/sh/v3@v3.14.1/expand).

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./intercept -run '^Test(RestrictiveRulesSeeStaticShellOperations|OperationSubjectsPreserveRuleControls|StaticOperationsCoverInteractiveAndResolvedExecutors)$' -count=1 -v -timeout 30s` (0.786s). Initial failures for wget canonical markers and backslash decoding were corrected without weakening assertions. Full `go test -p 1 ./intercept ./guard -count=1 -v -timeout 45s` passed (2.267s/0.931s), using isolated configuration and disposable PostgreSQL semantic_operations_20261010. Production Guard hooks loaded actual seeded rules and rejected quoted deletion/formatting and curl method variants; operator allow overrides and disabled interception were exercised through the same hooks. No provider/LLM calls were needed.

Passed: `go test ./server -run '^TestCustom(ConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule|.*Operation|.*Redirect.*|.*Resolved.*)$' -count=1 -timeout 60s` (2.864s), against separate disposable PostgreSQL semantic_sdk_operations_20261010. This covers existing SDK resolved-operation, configured-rule and redirect review tests; it is not a full server-suite claim. A source overlay removing only the added parsed-subject registration failed the identical quoted-command/DELETE regressions as expected (0.728s). The overlay is a fault injection, not an exact-HEAD baseline. Logs: semantic-operation-tests.log, semantic-operation-guard-tests.log, semantic-custom-operation-tests.log and semantic-operations-negative/result.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

Both families remain partial: wrapper executors, curl configuration files, variable/generated programs, incomplete interactive-shell fragments and target-side effects of arbitrary interpreters need a complete execution boundary. Static parsing is not a runtime sandbox and does not close REC-022. The two retained quoted-command/curl-spelling subcases now have verified repairs, while completion remains unproven for the broader control. Thirty families remain fixed and nine open. No additional paid scan worker, external target, real credential or provider request was used.

### Wrapper and literal-shell follow-up

Static projections now traverse supported command/exec/env/sudo/nohup/nice/timeout wrapper options and literal sh/bash/dash/ksh -c programs. Wrapper traversal consumes arguments iteratively, so repeated env wrappers do not evade an arbitrary recursion cutoff. Nested shell program parsing is bounded to eight levels and remains an explicit coverage limit. Environment assignment keys are preserved even when their values are dynamic, without expanding those values; this lets env PATH="$PATH" still review the following static executable. Tilde/home resolution is excluded. Listing/help modes, command -v/-V, shell -n syntax checks, script filenames and positional arguments are not reinterpreted as executable bodies. Unknown option semantics are not guessed. This remains a static review aid, not a sandbox.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test -p 1 ./intercept ./guard -count=1 -v -timeout 45s` (1.130s/0.954s), using the same isolated configuration and disposable semantic_operations_20261010 database. Added cases cover common sudo/env options, nested wrappers, twenty repeated env wrappers, dynamic assignment values, quoted shell -c programs and harmless data modes. Production Guard tests load actual seeded rules for sudo/env/bash wrappers. Existing policy-load, audit/trace, explicit allow, approval and disabled-control tests remain green.

Passed: `go test ./server -run '^TestCustom(ConfiguredCommandCannotBypassExecutorRule|.*Operation|.*Redirect.*|.*Resolved.*)$' -count=1 -timeout 60s` (2.168s), with the separate disposable semantic_sdk_operations_20261010 database. A fault-injection overlay disabling only wrapper traversal fails the unchanged wrapper regressions as expected. Logs: semantic-wrapper-tests.log, semantic-wrapper-sdk-tests.log and semantic-wrappers-negative/result.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

Seven positive real Bash/BusyBox fixture cases ran inside the already-existing disposable PostgreSQL container. Temporary harmless stub executables named rm/curl/mkfs.ext4 only print their received arguments; no destructive executable or network request was invoked. They validate quote concatenation and actual exec/env/nice/nohup/timeout/bash forwarding. command -pv checks lookup only. The first timeout case rejected GNU --signal because that fixture contains BusyBox; the failure is retained and its supported -s form passed. Sudo behavior is covered by static parsing/production guard tests, not live privilege execution. Fixture log: semantic-wrapper-bash-fixtures.log. Relevant semantics were checked against the [Bash builtin manual](https://www.gnu.org/s/bash/manual/html_node/Bash-Builtins.html) and [GNU env manual](https://www.gnu.org/s/coreutils/manual/html_node/env-invocation.html).

Remaining REC-004/034 gaps include unsupported wrapper modes/options, deep nested shells, generated programs, curl configuration files and incomplete interactive fragments. Thirty families remain fixed and nine open. No additional paid scan workers or provider requests were used.

## REC-020: verified assessment TLS and explicit target exceptions

Confirmed: both Python recon templates set CERT_NONE and disable hostname checking for all requests. The runtime template separately sets NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0 and launches Chromium with --ignore-certificate-errors. The recording proxy has SslInsecure=true, and enrichment constructs an InsecureSkipVerify transport. The MCP client's explicit configured insecure option is not a default bypass and is not changed here.

The Python harvester and crawler now use platform certificate/hostname verification by default. A shared tls_policy.py helper adds --ca-file for a PEM CA in addition to platform trust, preserving public bundle roots and hostname checks. The mutually exclusive --insecure option is explicit and applies only to the HTTPS base origin's exact scheme/hostname/port. The handler chooses a context anew for each request, including redirects and foreign bundles; foreign origins never inherit the exception. The crawler's existing same-origin cookie/redirect rules remain enforced. Rejected TLS responses are omitted and reported on stderr as coverage gaps. English/Korean recon documentation describes options and copying the new helper beside the scripts.

Passed: `python -m unittest discover -s skills/api-recon/scripts -p 'test_*.py' -v` (13 tests, 9.282s, no skips), including all earlier credential-origin and response-budget regressions. New stdlib-only tests use two actual loopback HTTPS listeners and a committed synthetic, public test certificate/key. They verify untrusted certificates rejected before any HTTP/cookie delivery, successful verified private-CA harvesting/authenticated crawling, rejected hostname mismatch even with trusted CA, same-origin redirects working with the explicit exception, foreign port/hostname/bundle/redirect rejection, and actual CLI default/CA/exception behavior for both scripts. Invalid exception origins and mutually exclusive options fail before requests. The exact-HEAD harvester loaded from an external baseline file fails the same default-rejection test as expected, delivering the synthetic HTML with status 200. No checkout reset or assertion changes were used. Logs: LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/recon-python-tls-tests.log and recon-tls-baseline/result.log. `git diff --check` passed.

The runtime recon subcase is now repaired too. Its process-global NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0 and Chromium --ignore-certificate-errors flag are removed. Node uses explicit verified Undici dispatchers, even if an ambient TLS bypass is set. config.json may explicitly set tls.insecure=true for the exact HTTPS base origin only. Selection happens for each manual-redirect request; a verified dispatcher handles foreign origins. HTTP(S) proxy forwarding uses separate target/proxy TLS settings, so the target exception cannot disable HTTPS proxy verification. Response streams still go through the existing finite budgets. TLS forwarding failures return 502 and record a coverage gap instead of a fabricated successful authorization response. Browser setup handles CDP certificate-error events by exact request origin, cancelling foreign errors; unsupported setup fails without a global fallback. Browser and dispatcher cleanup runs on runtime errors.

Passed: `node --test skills/api-recon/scripts/credential_policy.test.cjs skills/api-recon/scripts/response_budget.test.cjs skills/api-recon/scripts/preload_budget.test.cjs skills/api-recon/scripts/tls_policy.test.cjs` (20 tests, no skips, 5.830s), using the existing isolated browser dependencies and actual installed headless Chrome. New tests verify Node rejects untrusted credentials despite an ambient bypass; approved self-signed streaming works only with explicit configuration; foreign port/hostname requests fail; Chrome defaults reject the certificate, while an approved exception still rejects fetch/navigation to another port serving the exact same certificate; HTTP CONNECT forwarding preserves policy; HTTPS proxy certificates remain verified; and the actual runtime template preserves cookie delivery, API rewriting, raw response recording and secure default rejection. Existing browser credential/response-budget regressions also passed. The exact-HEAD runtime in an external baseline directory fails the same default-rejection integration as expected, sending fixture cookies and API requests without an explicit exception. No checkout reset or assertion change was used. Logs: recon-all-node-tls-tests.log and recon-runtime-tls-baseline/result.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

The scripts package adds pinned Undici 7.30.0 (Node >=20.18.1) because the native global fetch does not expose configurable request-scoped TLS transport by itself. The initial 7.22.0 candidate had advisory entries and was replaced before final verification. `npm audit --prefix <isolated browser dependencies> --omit=dev --json` reports no Undici advisory entry, but seven existing high entries remain in that isolated older browser dependency tree; this is not an all-dependencies-clean claim. TLS dispatcher options were checked against [official Undici ProxyAgent documentation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nodejs/undici/v7.29.1/docs/docs/api/ProxyAgent.md). Chrome's certificate-error commands are deprecated in the bundled official devtools-protocol schema; actual browser regressions passed for the installed version, and upgrades must retain those regressions rather than substituting a global ignore flag. English/Korean recon documentation describes the new helper, runtime requirements and explicit exception.

Enrichment's unconditional verification bypass is now replaced with a request-scoped transport. Direct probes use platform trust and TLS >=1.2. The production manager supplies its recording proxy's CA and exact loopback HTTP URL; that CA is added only for requests actually using that route. Capture/global-proxy selection is sampled once per request. Turning capture off restores ordinary direct verification; unrelated egress proxies cannot inherit the recording CA. Missing/invalid recording CA configuration fails closed on its route without disabling unrelated direct HTTP. Proxy parser errors do not echo credential-bearing input.

Earlier enrichment checkpoint (superseded by the native SDK repair below): real verification exposed a go-mitmproxy compatibility issue: IP clients omit SNI, and v1.9.2 calls GetCert with the empty SNI name, producing a certificate without IP SANs. The CA issuer does support IP SANs when given an IP; the defect is the empty-name call site, not its IP issuance algorithm. Published v1.9.3 was inspected and retains those empty-SNI call sites, so merely updating the dependency would not repair this. No dependency or module-cache edits were made. For IP requests on the exact pinned local HTTP recording route only, VerifyConnection checks certificate signatures, chain, validity and server-auth usage against that recording CA alone. Named certificates must match the target IP; only that local issuer's legacy empty-name/no-SAN certificate uses the compatibility path. Direct connections and other proxies never use it. This authenticates the local recording peer and relies on the selected proxy's CONNECT target for legacy IP naming; it does not establish upstream target authentication. The recording proxy's upstream bypass remains separately open.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./enrich -count=1 -v -timeout 30s` (four tests, 1.130s). Actual loopback TLS and an actual go-mitmproxy listener verify direct untrusted-target rejection before HTTP delivery, successful CA-verified IP/DNS recording-route probes, capture disable/re-enable, unrelated TLS-proxy rejection and missing-CA isolation. Certificate checks reject a foreign issuer, a named foreign hostname, a different IP and an absent certificate; the pinned legacy leaf and correctly named IP leaf work. The first fixture build used the wrong SDK method name and was corrected; the subsequent real test exposed the IP/SNI failure above before the compatibility repair. The prior HEAD enrichment implementation, with signature-only additions for the new optional argument, fails the same direct-rejection regression as expected. No test assertions were weakened.

Passed: `go test ./enrich ./traffic -count=1 -timeout 45s` (1.215s/5.371s) before the final restriction of CA trust routes to loopback HTTP, followed by the final full enrichment package above. `go test ./server -run '^$' -count=1` (1.570s), using explicit isolated config/unreachable test-only DSN, verifies compilation only. Logs: enrichment-tls-tests.log, enrichment-tls-baseline/result.log and enrichment-server-compile.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/. gofmt and `git diff --check` passed.

Earlier HTTP checkpoint (superseded by the native SDK repair below): the intercepted HTTP recording path validates the upstream handshake through the SDK's TlsEstablishedServer hook, before its HTTP client sends application bytes. It verifies the actual CONNECT hostname/IP, certificate chain, validity and server-auth usage against platform roots plus an optional private CA. Failure closes both connection ends and never enables passthrough. The SDK's SslInsecure option remains true because that hook needs the completed handshake and the SDK cannot perform native empty-SNI IP verification; this is manual post-handshake certificate validation for intercepted HTTP, not native SDK verification or complete enforcement of every SDK transport. An already flagged UseSeparateClient request is rejected, but later SDK alternate-client paths are not yet completely covered.

Startup TLSOptions are wired through the CLI, production manager and traffic.Open. `-target-tls-ca /path/ca.pem` adds private-PKI trust. `-target-tls-insecure-origins https://target.example:8443,https://127.0.0.1:9443` explicitly authorizes certificate bypass only for exact HTTPS recording origins, including port. The parser rejects user info, paths, queries, fragments and invalid ports, normalizes port/IP spellings and treats the default port as 443. These options are startup configuration rather than agent-authored request fields. Bad configuration is rejected before stores open. English/Korean README guidance describes scope and remaining transport limitations. Direct enrichment is separate and continues to require platform-trusted CAs.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic ./enrich ./cmd/skopos -count=1 -timeout 45s` (5.904s/1.237s/1.440s) with isolated config and an explicit unreachable test-only DSN. New real TCP tests use the production Traffic proxy and verify zero HTTP delivery for an untrusted target, a trusted certificate with hostname mismatch, and an exception for another port. Supplied private CA and exact-origin exception cases preserve raw response and actual captured SQLite exchange rows. Certificate failures do not populate the passthrough map. Configuration regressions reject ambiguous origins/invalid ports and missing CA files. The CLI's opt-in database authentication integration was not rerun with this unreachable DSN. A fault-injection source overlay removing only upstream guard registration fails the real default-rejection regression as expected; it is not an exact-HEAD baseline. Logs: recording-tls-tests.log and recording-tls-negative/result.log under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

Passed: `go test ./server -run '^$' -count=1` (1.335s), compilation only, and `go test ./server -run '^TestManagerRejectsInvalidRecordingTLSBeforeStores$' -count=1 -v` (1.392s). The startup regression checks errors omit credential markers and no data-store directory is created for invalid origin/CA configuration. Logs: recording-tls-server-compile.log and recording-tls-config-tests.log in the same local test directory. gofmt and `git diff --check` passed.

At that earlier checkpoint REC-020 remained partial: the SDK's WSS implementation opens a second TLS connection using its global SslInsecure flag without invoking TlsEstablishedServer, and alternate-client/mutated-flow transports still need a complete verification boundary. The SDK source was inspected to identify those paths; the HTTP regressions do not establish them fixed. Transparent passthrough leaves end-to-end TLS authentication to the client and does not forge a trusted proxy leaf. Twenty-nine families remain fixed and ten open. No external target, real credentials, provider request or additional Deep Scan worker was used.

### Final native TLS repair for REC-020

A checked-in local replacement of go-mitmproxy v1.9.2 now selects TLS configuration for each concrete upstream address on ordinary HTTP, alternate HTTP and the separate WSS connection. Skopos supplies platform/private-CA verification with SslInsecure=false; only explicitly configured exact-origin exceptions bypass target verification. Alternate HTTP authenticates an HTTPS egress proxy separately, even when the target has an exception. IP clients without SNI now receive certificates named for the concrete CONNECT host, with correct IP SANs. Enrichment uses ordinary certificate/hostname verification and no longer accepts empty-name legacy certificates. The earlier post-handshake hook remains as an additional check; the alternate-client denial is removed because the native transport now enforces policy.

The original module cache and root go.sum are unchanged. third_party/go-mitmproxy/UPSTREAM.json records original hashes and module checksum, LICENSE is retained, and README.local.md documents the three modified upstream files and required upgrade regressions. This local dependency patch must be retained or revalidated on upgrades.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic ./enrich -count=1 -timeout 45s` (6.971s/1.129s). `go test ./traffic -run '^TestNativeTLSProtects' -count=1 -v -timeout 45s` passed eight actual loopback WSS/alternate HTTP cases (1.966s): default untrusted rejection, private CA success, explicit-origin success, and rejection of a certificate swapped on the second TLS connection. A source overlay restoring only the WSS verification bypass fails the same second-connection regression as expected (unverified WSS connection accepted). No assertion changes or checkout reset were used.

Passed: `go test github.com/lqqyt2423/go-mitmproxy/proxy -run '^TestAlternateTLSSeparatesHTTPSProxyFromTargetException$' -count=1 -v -timeout 30s` (0.776s), verifying real trusted HTTPS CONNECT success and untrusted proxy rejection before CONNECT despite a target exception. Upstream regressions passed with `go test github.com/lqqyt2423/go-mitmproxy/proxy -run 'Test(WebSocket|SSE|HTTP2|AlternateTLS)' -count=1 -timeout 60s` (4.068s), followed by the correctly named HTTP2/connection tests with `-run 'Test(AttackerHTTP2|Connection|OffUpstreamCert|OnUpstreamCert)' -count=1 -timeout 45s` (1.339s).

Passed: `go test ./cmd/skopos ./server -run '^Test(ManagerRejectsInvalidRecordingTLSBeforeStores|ManagementTLS|DefaultManagement)' -count=1 -timeout 30s` (1.904s/1.438s), with explicit isolated configuration and an unreachable test-only DSN. ## REC-025: streaming traffic archive export

Confirmed: ExportHosts collected every exchange in memory, marshaled the entire manifest at once and read complete blob files before copying them. It also holds the capture writer lock throughout export. Exchange records now stream into a private temporary manifest, and blob files stream into private temporary files before publication. SQLite produces the sorted, deduplicated hash index without a collection-sized Go map or slice. The existing version-1 archive format, exact-host selection, timestamp/ID ordering, raw body bytes and blob deduplication are preserved. Export errors retain a previously published manifest and remove its incomplete temporary replacement.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic -count=1 -v` (4.351s), using the previously verified portable Go 1.26.9. The 16 MiB blob regression measured 58,056 bytes of Go allocation during export and verified the streamed blob's exact size and SHA-256. Multi-row ordering, byte preservation, missing-blob failure preservation and the existing archive round-trip/current-row-wins regressions passed. The same allocation regression against an exact-HEAD source overlay failed as expected with 16,828,304 allocated bytes before this repair. No checkout reset or assertion change was used. `git diff --check` passed. Final output is retained in LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/archive-stream-tests.log.

That first checkpoint left the writer-lock lifetime open. Export now establishes a SQLite WAL read snapshot under the short capture writer lock, then releases that lock before writing archive output. A separate file-retention read lock protects immutable blobs and legacy trees. Destructive entry points acquire its write side before the capture lock, so a deletion waiting for export cannot hold up capture. Staged commit, rollback, error exits and startup recovery release both locks; the simulated-process-exit fixture was updated to release the new lock too. Background WAL truncation also waits for retained snapshots before taking the capture lock. A joined exchange/body query avoids issuing nested queries on the snapshot connection.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test ./traffic -count=1 -v -timeout 45s` (5.545s). A deliberately stalled blob-copy regression verifies actual capture commits while each of substring deletion, clear-all, exact-host staged commit, staged rollback and startup recovery waits. The published archive excludes the later capture and its distinct blob, retains the original raw blob/legacy data, and each subsequent delete/rollback preserves its expected semantics. An initially empty snapshot also excludes a later successful capture. The full package includes archive round-trip, current-row-wins and crash recovery regressions. The final 16 MiB export measured 57,056 allocated Go bytes. A fault-injection source overlay restoring the long writer-lock lifetime fails the concurrency regression as expected; it is not an exact-HEAD baseline. Output is retained in LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/archive-snapshot-tests.log and archive-lock-negative/result.log. The first run hung because the old crash simulation left the newly introduced lock held; that identified test process was stopped and the helper corrected without changing assertions. The regression now joins its goroutines on failure too. `go test ./server -run '^$' -count=1` passed (1.680s) with an explicit unreachable test-only DSN; this verifies compilation only, not server integration. `git diff --check` passed.

Individual legacy HTTP files are still read whole, archive import still buffers its manifest/bodies, and SQLite temporary storage/WAL growth has no new disk quota. These are distinct from this family's whole-collection export allocation and capture-lock lifetime; storage quotas remain open under REC-024. REC-025 is fixed; 29 families are fixed and 10 remain open. All verification used local synthetic traffic without scan/model workers.

## REC-018: management transport, default exposure and native HTTPS

Confirmed: the binary's default :8787 listener and Compose's published 8787 port were network-visible HTTP. The credential-cookie subcase has already been removed by REC-026/035: the API rejects cookies, JWTs use explicit request credentials, and the remaining route cookie contains only a non-secret hint. HTTP can still expose a password or explicit bearer token and must be addressed independently.

The binary now defaults to 127.0.0.1:8787. Compose host publication is loopback-only, preserving its internal container listener and local workflow. dev.sh binds both backend and Next.js to loopback. Native HTTPS accepts -tls-cert/-tls-key, validates the pair before manager/store initialization, preloads the certificate and requires TLS 1.2 or newer. A selected HTTPS listener serves TLS exclusively, without a plaintext fallback. English/Korean documentation covers local defaults and explicit native/ingress HTTPS deployment.

Passed: `go test ./cmd/skopos -count=1` (0.966s), with isolated config and unreachable test-only DSN. The new real TCP test verifies loopback defaults, unchanged local HTTP selection, invalid/missing certificate pairs failing, a synthetic credential POST reaching a native HTTPS fixture handler with TLS state, and a plaintext HTTP request never reaching that handler. This is a transport test, not the full application authentication workflow. No real password, provider or database was used.

Passed: `POSTGRES_PASSWORD=compose-validation-only docker compose config --format json` followed by JSON inspection resolves both published management/recording ports to host_ip 127.0.0.1. This validates configuration only; no application container was rebuilt or deployed. gofmt and git diff --check passed. The development shell script was edited but not launched on Windows.

Application HTTPS verification now passes: `SKOPOS_TLS_E2E=1 go test ./cmd/skopos -count=1 -v` (3.088s), explicitly selecting fresh disposable skopos_transport_security through SKOPOS_PG_DSN and an isolated config. TestNativeHTTPSManagementAuthentication constructs the production manager/server, reads the actual local enrollment token, loads certificate files through the production TLS helper and serves the real API over TCP/TLS. It checks rejected invalid setup tokens, successful enrollment, unauthorized reads without a bearer token, authorized settings reads, login, password change, revocation of both earlier tokens, replacement-password login and its authorized read. Every response must have TLS connection state; credentials are synthetic and never printed. The database has no provider profiles, external MCPs are disabled and the engine reports idle. Both earlier CLI tests also passed without skips. gofmt and git diff --check passed.

The existing agent adapter already accepts only HTTPS or literal loopback HTTP origins. The web API client now applies the same policy before accessing localStorage credentials or sending a password request. Its EventSource URL builder also rejects an explicitly configured remote plaintext destination before attaching a token. Mock/demo transport is preserved. A typed transport error lets login/setup distinguish the HTTPS requirement from ordinary authentication failure; it is translated at render time so persisted Korean and subsequent language changes do not retain an English startup error.

Passed: `npm.cmd test` in web/ (all 29 tests, zero skips, 1.168s), including the real API module with real locale/request modules and a synthetic fetch sink. Negative cases cover initialization, login, password change, authenticated API requests and SSE construction: remote HTTP produces zero credential reads and zero requests. Positive loopback/HTTPS calls preserve bearer headers; remote HTTP SSE configuration is rejected. Origin tests reject hostname prefixes, private remote IPs, userinfo, invalid schemes and malformed URLs.

Passed: `NEXT_EXPORT=1 npm.cmd run build` in web/ after the final rendering change: compilation, TypeScript and all 31 exported routes. Actual compiled login/setup Chromium verification passed 24 cases across English/Korean, widths 1440/390 and three transports. A remote-looking DNS hostname was mapped to a local HTTP fixture; all eight HTTP cases showed the localized HTTPS error and emitted zero API/credential POSTs, including form submission. Eight literal-loopback HTTP and eight HTTPS cases kept status checks and form submission working. No layout overflow was observed. The HTTPS fixture uses a synthetic self-signed certificate with browser trust checks disabled for that fixture only; this proves scheme/flow behavior, not production certificate trust. The earlier Go HTTPS integration validates its trusted fixture certificate normally. The browser first caught an English-only startup message for Korean; typed error retention and render-time translation fixed it without weakening the language assertion.

Verification artifacts outside the repository: management-transport-web-tests.log, management-transport-web-build.log, management-transport-ui.cjs and management-transport-ui-results.json under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/. All UI responses/credentials were synthetic; no real service or model provider was called. git diff --check passed.

At the preceding checkpoint, REC-018 remained open: an explicitly configured HTTP listener and the internal Docker network still permit plaintext traffic from clients outside the guarded web/agent clients. These defaults, HTTPS support and client checks do not establish mandatory backend remote TLS or prove an operator's ingress/network isolation. Further deployment-path enforcement is required before claiming this family fixed. The integrations do not launch the built CLI process or verify an external reverse proxy or actual remote deployment.


### Mandatory network TLS and Docker migration

Network-visible management HTTP is now rejected before self-update bootstrap, manager/store initialization or listener startup. Only a resolved loopback address permits HTTP. The validated HTTP address is pinned to the actual IP, preserving localhost while avoiding a later hostname resolution changing the bind address. Native HTTPS continues to validate/load the certificate pair first and requires TLS 1.2 or newer. Local development and same-host TLS reverse proxies can retain loopback HTTP; the external ingress must use HTTPS, and that external deployment is operator-controlled rather than verified by the backend. Forwarded headers do not grant permission for a remote HTTP listener.

Docker's default command now supplies -tls-cert /app/tls/server.pem and -tls-key /app/tls/server-key.pem. Compose keeps loopback host publication and mounts ./tls read-only. Certificate files are excluded from Git and image build context. The bilingual installer rejects missing files before Docker detection/build/start and reports the HTTPS URL. Existing Docker deployments require a trusted, hostname-valid certificate pair before restart and must switch to HTTPS; browser storage changes origin, so login is required again. README instructions and .env.example document this migration. No certificate verification bypass or remote plaintext override was introduced; missing/invalid TLS fails startup. Recording-proxy authorization remains separately open as REC-027.

Passed: `GOTOOLCHAIN=local SKOPOS_TLS_E2E=1 go test ./cmd/skopos -count=1 -v -timeout 45s` (2.102s), with SKOPOS_CONFIG pointing to an isolated absent config and SKOPOS_PG_DSN selecting disposable skopos_transport_security. All TLS tests pass, including real application enrollment/login/password change/revocation over trusted HTTPS, loopback/IPv6/mapped-address preservation, wildcard/remote HTTP rejection and HTTPS listener acceptance. The independent password-reset PostgreSQL opt-in test skipped; this run does not repeat that family's full validation. Log: management-remote-tls-tests.log.

Built the actual Windows CLI and exercised four rejected network HTTP listeners, asserting failure before data-directory creation; local localhost HTTP remains functional. A wildcard native HTTPS listener served health through certificate-verified TLS, and plaintext received the TLS listener's rejection. Cross-built the Linux CLI with CGO_ENABLED=0 and exercised it in the existing disposable PostgreSQL container, without publishing another port: network HTTP rejected, wildcard HTTPS certificate verification passed, missing authentication returned 401, and plaintext returned 400. These are real CLI/TCP/TLS processes and a local synthetic certificate, not mocked request handlers. The initial copy of an absent config failed; a fixture config was then created/copied and the container check rerun successfully. Artifacts: management-cli-fixture/{verify.py,results.json,linux_verify.go,linux-results.json,native process logs} under LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/.

Passed: `POSTGRES_PASSWORD=compose-validation-only docker compose config --format json`; JSON assertions confirm host loopback publication and read-only /app/tls mount. `bash -n install.sh start.sh` passed. Four copied installer fixtures cover English/Korean with/without files; Docker is explicitly a synthetic stub in those fixtures. Missing files cause zero Docker commands; present files retain build/start and report HTTPS. Logs/scripts: management-cli-fixture/installer_verify.py and installer-results.json. The complete Docker image was not rebuilt or deployed, and no external ingress/browser certificate installation was performed. Frontend code is unchanged in this follow-up; its earlier compiled HTTPS UI checks remain above. gofmt and `git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check` passed.

A fault-injection source overlay disabling only the loopback restriction fails the identical remote-listener regression (2.031s), accepting :8787 as expected from the faulty variant. Log: management-remote-negative/result.log. No listener is started by that regression. This is not an exact-HEAD baseline.

REC-018 is fixed at the application listener/configuration boundary. Thirty-two families are fixed and seven remain open. All credentials, certificates and processes used here were disposable local fixtures; no production deployment or paid scan/provider worker ran.

## REC-015: reconnaissance response and capture budgets

Confirmed: static bundle harvesting and the MPA crawler used unbounded response reads. The static reader also downloaded manifest expansions concurrently without a shared response-byte or attempted-request allowance.
