## REC-016: authentication request budgets

Current code confirmed unbounded JSON decoding on initialization, login, and password changes. These routes now have an 8 KiB body budget and a 10-second body-read deadline. Trailing JSON and oversized trailing whitespace are rejected. Passwords above the existing bcrypt 72-byte maximum receive an explicit client error. Limits are route-specific to preserve streaming and larger upload bodies.

Regression tests cover byte boundaries, unknown fields, trailing whitespace, multiple JSON values, invalid JSON, all three routed endpoints, and a real slow TCP request through the locale middleware.

## REC-001: first-owner enrollment

Initialization now requires a setup token proving access to the backend installation. The local executable's -setup-token option derives it from the existing signing key with a domain-separated HMAC, without revealing the signing key, changing files, starting another server, or contacting PostgreSQL. No token is returned by the public status API. Missing, invalid, and different-installation tokens are rejected before database work. The English and Korean setup forms, API client, adapter fixture and installation documentation were updated. Existing password-based login is unchanged.

Initialization uses a conditional PostgreSQL upsert. Only a missing or empty password setting can be initialized, and only the winning request receives a JWT. Database read errors fail closed. Concurrent enrollment losers can return 403 from the atomic ownership condition or 429 before hashing from the password-work budget; the regression explicitly checks exactly one successful owner. This replaces the previous unconditional upsert and unbounded hashing behavior rather than relaxing the ownership assertion.

## REC-017: login and password-work admission

Login has a per-direct-peer bucket (five immediate attempts, one replenished every six seconds), and a global bucket (twenty immediate attempts, one replenished every three seconds). Forwarded identity headers do not change the direct peer allowance. Peer entries are capped at 1024 and expired entries are reclaimed after ten minutes. A reverse proxy consequently shares one peer allowance among its clients; this suits the single-administrator console and does not silently trust proxy headers. Limits are per server process, not a distributed rate limiter.

At most two password-hashing/comparison jobs run simultaneously across setup, login and password changes, with no waiting request queue. Busy responses return 429 and Retry-After: 6. Deterministic tests verify burst rejection, refill, distributed-source limits, bounded peer storage, ignored forwarded identity, concurrent password-work caps and release. Valid enrollment, login and password changes are exercised against disposable PostgreSQL.

Passed: go test ./server ./cmd/skopos -run '^Test(DecodeAuthRequestBounds|AuthRoutesRejectOversizedBodiesBeforeDatabaseWork|AuthBodyDeadlineBoundsSlowRequest|AuthEnrollmentAndPasswordFlowPG|AuthLoginPasswordByteBoundary|LoginAdmission.*|PasswordWorkConcurrencyBudget|LoginRateLimitIgnoresForwardedIdentity|ReadSetupTokenRequiresExistingLocalKey)$' -count=1. Server: 13.700 seconds. cmd/skopos compiled successfully; no matching tests existed there.

Frontend dependencies were installed with npm ci --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund in web/; no dependency versions or manifests changed. Normalizing the relative path to slash separators preserves the exact existing exceptions and assertions. Final npm test: all 21 tests passed (1.004 seconds).

Passed: NEXT_EXPORT=1 npm run build, set through the PowerShell NEXT_EXPORT environment variable before npm.cmd run build. Production compilation, TypeScript checking and all 31 static pages completed successfully. Build and test output are retained in LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/frontend-build.log and frontend-tests.log. Rendered browser checks were not performed.

Passed: go build -o <isolated-setup-cli>/skopos.exe ./cmd/skopos, followed by skopos.exe -setup-token using a fixture key next to that binary outside the repository. Exactly one correctly shaped token was returned and the key SHA-256 remained unchanged; the token was not displayed in logs.

Passed: SKOPOS_ADAPTER_E2E=1 go test ./server -run '^TestAgentAdapterE2E$' -count=1 -v on a fresh skopos_adapter_security database (9.469 seconds). All three actual MCP/Pi adapter integration cases passed against the real authenticated backend: task creation/controls/coverage/findings, authentication and invalid-input failures/read-only mode, and Pi task/evidence operations. ## Verification

Passed: go test ./server -run '^Test(DecodeAuthRequestBounds|AuthRoutesRejectOversizedBodiesBeforeDatabaseWork|AuthBodyDeadlineBoundsSlowRequest)$' -count=1 (13.034 seconds). SKOPOS_CONFIG was overridden to an isolated nonexistent config and SKOPOS_PG_DSN to an unreachable loopback address.

Passed: go test ./server -run '^Test(DecodeAuthRequestBounds|AuthRoutesRejectOversizedBodiesBeforeDatabaseWork|AuthBodyDeadlineBoundsSlowRequest|AuthEnrollmentAndPasswordFlowPG|AuthLoginPasswordByteBoundary)$' -count=1 (13.207 seconds). The disposable database regression ran, without skipping, and verified exactly one winner among eight concurrent enrollment requests, login, password change, rejection of the previous password, and login with the replacement password. ASCII and UTF-8 password byte boundaries were also checked.

Full package check: go test ./server -count=1 -json failed (67.204 seconds), with 235 top-level tests passing, seven failing, and two skipped. The adapter E2E/provider-specific checks were not enabled. Detailed output is retained outside the repository at LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/server-remediation-tests.jsonl.

Baseline comparison: a Go build overlay substituted the exact HEAD versions of server/auth.go, db/settings.go, and locale/api.go and replaced the new test file with an empty package declaration. This did not modify the working tree or create another checkout. All seven failing tests reproduced on the original code against a separate disposable database (8.455 seconds):

- TestFindingTrafficAPIAndExport
- TestFindingTrafficArchiveV3RoundTripAndRetry
- TestFindingTrafficUTF8SegmentsAndInheritedWrites
- TestFindingLocalizedEvidencePreservesRawBytes
- TestFindingWorkflowAutoHintToPlannerAndSetting
- TestFindingWorkflowReporterBindsBeforeWritingReport
- TestSkillRelPath

The first six report Windows access-denied errors when syncing evidence directories. The last expects slash-separated skill paths but receives Windows backslashes. These pre-existing Windows compatibility issues remain open; assertions were not altered. The baseline command was go test -overlay <baseline-overlay/overlay.json> ./server -run '^Test(FindingTrafficAPIAndExport|FindingTrafficArchiveV3RoundTripAndRetry|FindingTrafficUTF8SegmentsAndInheritedWrites|FindingLocalizedEvidencePreservesRawBytes|FindingWorkflowAutoHintToPlannerAndSetting|FindingWorkflowReporterBindsBeforeWritingReport|SkillRelPath)$' -count=1 -json. Baseline output is retained at LOCAL_SECURITY_ARTIFACTS/server-baseline-tests.jsonl.

gofmt ran on changed Go files. git diff --check passed. No production database or real model provider was used in the focused regressions; existing full-suite model fixtures remain test-only.

Portable Go 1.26.9, official archive SHA-256 verified before extraction. PostgreSQL 16 Alpine is running in a disposable Docker container with a loopback-only published port and test-only credentials. Existing root node_modules, package.json, and package-lock.json are unrelated and preserved.

No additional Deep Scan workers were started for remediation.
